```
1→"""Concern generators — mechanical findings → subjective review bridge.
     2→
     3→Concerns are ephemeral: computed on-demand from current state, never persisted.
     4→Only LLM-confirmed concerns become persistent Finding objects via review import.
     5→
     6→Generators focus on cross-cutting synthesis — bundling all signals per file so
     7→the LLM gets a complete picture, and surfacing systemic patterns across files
     8→that no single detector captures.
     9→"""
    10→
    11→from __future__ import annotations
    12→
    13→import hashlib
    14→from collections import defaultdict
    15→from dataclasses import dataclass
    16→from typing import Any, Literal, TypedDict, cast
    17→
    18→from desloppify.core.registry import JUDGMENT_DETECTORS
    19→
    20→
    21→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    22→class Concern:
    23→    """A potential design problem surfaced by mechanical signals."""
    24→
    25→    type: str  # concern classification
    26→    file: str  # primary file (relative path)
    27→    summary: str  # human-readable 1-liner
    28→    evidence: tuple[str, ...]  # supporting data points
    29→    question: str  # specific question for LLM to evaluate
    30→    fingerprint: str  # stable hash for dismissal tracking
    31→    source_findings: tuple[str, ...]  # finding IDs that triggered this
    32→
    33→
    34→class ConcernSignals(TypedDict, total=False):
    35→    """Typed signal payload extracted from mechanical findings."""
    36→
    37→    max_params: float
    38→    max_nesting: float
    39→    loc: float
    40→    function_count: float
    41→    monster_loc: float
    42→    monster_funcs: list[str]
    43→
    44→
    45→_NUMERIC_SIGNAL_KEYS = ("max_params", "max_nesting", "loc", "function_count")
    46→SignalKey = Literal["max_params", "max_nesting", "loc", "function_count", "monster_loc"]
    47→
    48→
    49→def _update_max_signal(signals: ConcernSignals, key: SignalKey, value: object) -> None:
    50→    """Update numeric signal key with max(existing, value) when value is valid."""
    51→    if isinstance(value, bool) or not isinstance(value, int | float) or value <= 0:
    52→        return
    53→    current = float(signals.get(key, 0.0))
    54→    signals[key] = max(current, float(value))
    55→
    56→
    57→def _fingerprint(concern_type: str, file: str, key_signals: tuple[str, ...]) -> str:
    58→    """Stable hash of (type, file, sorted key signals)."""
    59→    raw = f"{concern_type}::{file}::{','.join(sorted(key_signals))}"
    60→    return hashlib.sha256(raw.encode()).hexdigest()[:16]
    61→
    62→
    63→def _is_dismissed(
    64→    dismissals: dict, fingerprint: str, source_finding_ids: tuple[str, ...]
    65→) -> bool:
    66→    """Check if a concern was previously dismissed and source findings unchanged."""
    67→    entry = dismissals.get(fingerprint)
    68→    if not isinstance(entry, dict):
    69→        return False
    70→    prev_sources = set(entry.get("source_finding_ids", []))
    71→    return prev_sources == set(source_finding_ids)
    72→
    73→
    74→def _open_findings(state: dict[str, Any]) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
    75→    """Return all open findings from state."""
    76→    findings = state.get("findings", {})
    77→    return [
    78→        f for f in findings.values()
    79→        if isinstance(f, dict) and f.get("status") == "open"
    80→    ]
    81→
    82→
    83→def _group_by_file(state: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, list[dict[str, Any]]]:
    84→    """Group open findings by file, excluding holistic (file='.')."""
    85→    by_file: dict[str, list[dict[str, Any]]] = defaultdict(list)
    86→    for f in _open_findings(state):
    87→        file = f.get("file", "")
    88→        if file and file != ".":
    89→            by_file[file].append(f)
    90→    return dict(by_file)
    91→
    92→
    93→# ── Signal extraction ────────────────────────────────────────────────
    94→
    95→
    96→def _extract_signals(findings: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> ConcernSignals:
    97→    """Extract key quantitative signals from a file's findings."""
    98→    signals: ConcernSignals = {}
    99→    monster_funcs: list[str] = []
   100→
   101→    for f in findings:
   102→        det = f.get("detector", "")
   103→        detail_raw = f.get("detail", {})
   104→        detail = detail_raw if isinstance(detail_raw, dict) else {}
   105→
   106→        if det == "structural":
   107→            s = detail.get("signals", {})
   108→            if isinstance(s, dict):
   109→                for key in _NUMERIC_SIGNAL_KEYS:
   110→                    _update_max_signal(signals, cast(SignalKey, key), s.get(key, 0))
   111→
   112→        if det == "smells" and detail.get("smell_id") == "monster_function":
   113→            _update_max_signal(signals, "monster_loc", detail.get("loc", 0))
   114→            func = detail.get("function", "")
   115→            if isinstance(func, str) and func:
   116→                monster_funcs.append(func)
   117→
   118→    if monster_funcs:
   119→        signals["monster_funcs"] = monster_funcs
   120→    return signals
   121→
   122→
   123→def _has_elevated_signals(findings: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> bool:
   124→    """Does any finding have signals strong enough to flag on its own?"""
   125→    for f in findings:
   126→        det = f.get("detector", "")
   127→        detail = f.get("detail", {})
   128→
   129→        if det == "structural":
   130→            s = detail.get("signals", {})
   131→            if isinstance(s, dict):
   132→                if s.get("max_params", 0) >= 8:
   133→                    return True
   134→                if s.get("max_nesting", 0) >= 6:
   135→                    return True
   136→                if s.get("loc", 0) >= 300:
   137→                    return True
   138→
   139→        if det == "smells" and detail.get("smell_id") == "monster_function":
   140→            return True
   141→
   142→        if det in ("dupes", "boilerplate_duplication", "coupling",
   143→                    "responsibility_cohesion"):
   144→            return True
   145→
   146→    return False
   147→
   148→
   149→# ── Concern classification ───────────────────────────────────────────
   150→
   151→
   152→def _classify(detectors: set[str], signals: ConcernSignals) -> str:
   153→    """Pick the most specific concern type from what's present."""
   154→    if len(detectors) >= 3:
   155→        return "mixed_responsibilities"
   156→    if "dupes" in detectors or "boilerplate_duplication" in detectors:
   157→        return "duplication_design"
   158→    if signals.get("monster_loc", 0) > 0:
   159→        return "structural_complexity"
   160→    if "coupling" in detectors:
   161→        return "coupling_design"
   162→    if signals.get("max_params", 0) >= 8:
   163→        return "interface_design"
   164→    if signals.get("max_nesting", 0) >= 6:
   165→        return "structural_complexity"
   166→    if "responsibility_cohesion" in detectors:
   167→        return "mixed_responsibilities"
   168→    return "design_concern"
   169→
   170→
   171→def _build_summary(
   172→    concern_type: str,
   173→    detectors: set[str],
   174→    signals: ConcernSignals,
   175→) -> str:
   176→    """Human-readable one-liner."""
   177→    if concern_type == "mixed_responsibilities":
   178→        return (
   179→            f"Issues from {len(detectors)} detectors — "
   180→            "may have too many responsibilities"
   181→        )
   182→    if concern_type == "structural_complexity":
   183→        parts: list[str] = []
   184→        monster_loc = signals.get("monster_loc", 0)
   185→        if monster_loc:
   186→            funcs = signals.get("monster_funcs", [])
   187→            label = f" ({', '.join(funcs[:3])})" if funcs else ""
   188→            parts.append(f"monster function{label}: {int(monster_loc)} lines")
   189→        nesting = signals.get("max_nesting", 0)
   190→        if nesting >= 6:
   191→            parts.append(f"nesting depth {int(nesting)}")
   192→        params = signals.get("max_params", 0)
   193→        if params >= 8:
   194→            parts.append(f"{int(params)} parameters")
   195→        return f"Structural complexity: {', '.join(parts) or 'elevated signals'}"
   196→    if concern_type == "duplication_design":
   197→        return "Duplication pattern — assess if extraction is warranted"
   198→    if concern_type == "coupling_design":
   199→        return "Coupling pattern — assess if boundaries need adjustment"
   200→    if concern_type == "interface_design":
   201→        return f"Interface complexity: {int(signals.get('max_params', 0))} parameters"
   202→    return f"Design signals from {', '.join(sorted(detectors))}"
   203→
   204→
   205→def _build_evidence(
   206→    findings: list[dict[str, Any]],
   207→    signals: ConcernSignals,
   208→) -> tuple[str, ...]:
   209→    """Build evidence tuple from all findings and extracted signals."""
   210→    evidence: list[str] = []
   211→
   212→    detectors = sorted({f.get("detector", "") for f in findings})
   213→    evidence.append(f"Flagged by: {', '.join(detectors)}")
   214→
   215→    loc = signals.get("loc")
   216→    if loc:
   217→        evidence.append(f"File size: {int(loc)} lines")
   218→    params = signals.get("max_params")
   219→    if params and params >= 8:
   220→        evidence.append(f"Max parameters: {int(params)}")
   221→    nesting = signals.get("max_nesting")
   222→    if nesting and nesting >= 6:
   223→        evidence.append(f"Max nesting depth: {int(nesting)}")
   224→    monster_loc = signals.get("monster_loc")
   225→    if monster_loc:
   226→        funcs = signals.get("monster_funcs", [])
   227→        label = f" ({', '.join(funcs[:3])})" if funcs else ""
   228→        evidence.append(f"Monster function{label}: {int(monster_loc)} lines")
   229→
   230→    # Individual finding summaries — give LLM the full picture, capped.
   231→    for f in findings[:10]:
   232→        summary = f.get("summary", "")
   233→        if summary:
   234→            evidence.append(f"[{f.get('detector', '')}] {summary}")
   235→
   236→    return tuple(evidence)
   237→
   238→
   239→def _build_question(
   240→    detectors: set[str], signals: ConcernSignals
   241→) -> str:
   242→    """Build targeted question from dominant signals."""
   243→    parts: list[str] = []
   244→
   245→    if len(detectors) >= 3:
   246→        parts.append(
   247→            f"This file has issues across {len(detectors)} dimensions "
   248→            f"({', '.join(sorted(detectors))}). Is it trying to do too many "
   249→            "things, or is this complexity inherent to its domain?"
   250→        )
   251→
   252→    funcs = signals.get("monster_funcs", [])
   253→    if funcs:
   254→        parts.append(
   255→            f"What are the distinct responsibilities in {funcs[0]}()? "
   256→            "Should it be decomposed into focused functions?"
   257→        )
   258→
   259→    if signals.get("max_params", 0) >= 8:
   260→        parts.append(
   261→            "Should the parameters be grouped into a config/context object? "
   262→            "Which ones belong together?"
   263→        )
   264→
   265→    if signals.get("max_nesting", 0) >= 6:
   266→        parts.append(
   267→            "Can the nesting be reduced with early returns, guard clauses, "
   268→            "or extraction into helper functions?"
   269→        )
   270→
   271→    if "dupes" in detectors or "boilerplate_duplication" in detectors:
   272→        parts.append(
   273→            "Is the duplication worth extracting into a shared utility, "
   274→            "or is it intentional variation?"
   275→        )
   276→
   277→    if "coupling" in detectors:
   278→        parts.append(
   279→            "Is the coupling intentional or does it indicate a missing "
   280→            "abstraction boundary?"
   281→        )
   282→
   283→    if "orphaned" in detectors:
   284→        parts.append(
   285→            "Is this file truly dead, or is it used via a non-import mechanism "
   286→            "(dynamic import, CLI entry point, plugin)?"
   287→        )
   288→
   289→    if "responsibility_cohesion" in detectors:
   290→        parts.append(
   291→            "What are the distinct responsibilities? Should this module be "
   292→            "split along those lines?"
   293→        )
   294→
   295→    if not parts:
   296→        parts.append(
   297→            "Review the flagged patterns — are they design problems that "
   298→            "need addressing, or acceptable given the file's role?"
   299→        )
   300→
   301→    return " ".join(parts)
   302→
   303→
   304→# ── Generators ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   305→
   306→
   307→def _file_concerns(state: dict[str, Any], dismissals: dict[str, Any]) -> list[Concern]:
   308→    """Per-file design concerns from aggregated mechanical signals.
   309→
   310→    Flags a file if it has 2+ judgment-needed detectors OR a single
   311→    detector with elevated signals (monster function, high params,
   312→    deep nesting, duplication, coupling, mixed responsibilities).
   313→    Bundles ALL findings for that file so the LLM sees the full picture.
   314→    """
   315→    by_file = _group_by_file(state)
   316→    concerns: list[Concern] = []
   317→
   318→    for file, all_findings in by_file.items():
   319→        judgment = [
   320→            f for f in all_findings
   321→            if f.get("detector", "") in JUDGMENT_DETECTORS
   322→        ]
   323→        if not judgment:
   324→            continue
   325→
   326→        judgment_dets = {f.get("detector", "") for f in judgment}
   327→        elevated = _has_elevated_signals(judgment)
   328→
   329→        # Flag if 2+ judgment detectors OR 1 with elevated signals.
   330→        if len(judgment_dets) < 2 and not elevated:
   331→            continue
   332→
   333→        signals = _extract_signals(judgment)
   334→        concern_type = _classify(judgment_dets, signals)
   335→        evidence = _build_evidence(judgment, signals)
   336→        question = _build_question(judgment_dets, signals)
   337→        summary = _build_summary(concern_type, judgment_dets, signals)
   338→
   339→        all_ids = tuple(sorted(f.get("id", "") for f in judgment))
   340→        fp_keys = tuple(sorted(judgment_dets))
   341→        fp = _fingerprint(concern_type, file, fp_keys)
   342→
   343→        if _is_dismissed(dismissals, fp, all_ids):
   344→            continue
   345→
   346→        concerns.append(
   347→            Concern(
   348→                type=concern_type,
   349→                file=file,
   350→                summary=summary,
   351→                evidence=evidence,
   352→                question=question,
   353→                fingerprint=fp,
   354→                source_findings=all_ids,
   355→            )
   356→        )
   357→
   358→    return concerns
   359→
   360→
   361→def _cross_file_patterns(state: dict[str, Any], dismissals: dict[str, Any]) -> list[Concern]:
   362→    """Systemic patterns: same judgment detector combo across 3+ files.
   363→
   364→    When multiple files share the same combination of detector types,
   365→    that's likely a codebase-wide pattern rather than isolated issues.
   366→    """
   367→    by_file = _group_by_file(state)
   368→
   369→    # Group files by their judgment detector profile.
   370→    profile_to_files: dict[frozenset[str], list[str]] = defaultdict(list)
   371→    for file, findings in by_file.items():
   372→        dets = frozenset(
   373→            f.get("detector", "") for f in findings
   374→            if f.get("detector", "") in JUDGMENT_DETECTORS
   375→        )
   376→        if len(dets) >= 2:
   377→            profile_to_files[dets].append(file)
   378→
   379→    concerns: list[Concern] = []
   380→    for det_combo, files in profile_to_files.items():
   381→        if len(files) < 3:
   382→            continue
   383→
   384→        sorted_files = sorted(files)
   385→        combo_names = tuple(sorted(det_combo))
   386→        all_ids = tuple(sorted(
   387→            f.get("id", "")
   388→            for file in sorted_files
   389→            for f in by_file[file]
   390→            if f.get("detector", "") in det_combo
   391→        ))
   392→        # Use first few files in fingerprint so it's stable but bounded.
   393→        fp = _fingerprint(
   394→            "systemic_pattern",
   395→            ",".join(sorted_files[:5]),
   396→            combo_names,
   397→        )
   398→
   399→        if _is_dismissed(dismissals, fp, all_ids):
   400→            continue
   401→
   402→        concerns.append(
   403→            Concern(
   404→                type="systemic_pattern",
   405→                file=sorted_files[0],
   406→                summary=(
   407→                    f"{len(files)} files share the same problem pattern "
   408→                    f"({', '.join(combo_names)})"
   409→                ),
   410→                evidence=(
   411→                    f"Affected files: {', '.join(sorted_files[:10])}",
   412→                    f"Shared detectors: {', '.join(combo_names)}",
   413→                    f"Total files: {len(files)}",
   414→                ),
   415→                question=(
   416→                    f"These {len(files)} files all have the same combination "
   417→                    f"of issues ({', '.join(combo_names)}). Is this a systemic "
   418→                    "pattern that should be addressed at the architecture level "
   419→                    "(shared base class, framework change, lint rule), or are "
   420→                    "these independent issues that happen to look similar?"
   421→                ),
   422→                fingerprint=fp,
   423→                source_findings=all_ids,
   424→            )
   425→        )
   426→
   427→    return concerns
   428→
   429→
   430→_GENERATORS = [_file_concerns, _cross_file_patterns]
   431→
   432→
   433→def generate_concerns(
   434→    state: dict[str, Any],
   435→    lang_name: str | None = None,
   436→) -> list[Concern]:
   437→    """Run all concern generators against current state.
   438→
   439→    Returns deduplicated list sorted by (type, file).
   440→    lang_name is reserved for future language-specific generators.
   441→    """
   442→    del lang_name  # Reserved for future use.
   443→    dismissals = state.get("concern_dismissals", {})
   444→    concerns: list[Concern] = []
   445→    seen_fps: set[str] = set()
   446→
   447→    for gen in _GENERATORS:
   448→        for concern in gen(state, dismissals):
   449→            if concern.fingerprint not in seen_fps:
   450→                seen_fps.add(concern.fingerprint)
   451→                concerns.append(concern)
   452→
   453→    concerns.sort(key=lambda c: (c.type, c.file))
   454→    return concerns
   455→
   456→
   457→def cleanup_stale_dismissals(state: dict[str, Any]) -> int:
   458→    """Remove dismissals whose source findings all disappeared.
   459→
   460→    Returns the number of stale entries removed.  Dismissals without
   461→    ``source_finding_ids`` (legacy) are left untouched.
   462→    """
   463→    dismissals = state.get("concern_dismissals", {})
   464→    if not dismissals:
   465→        return 0
   466→    open_ids = {f.get("id", "") for f in _open_findings(state)}
   467→    stale_fps = [
   468→        fp
   469→        for fp, entry in dismissals.items()
   470→        if entry.get("source_finding_ids")
   471→        and not any(sid in open_ids for sid in entry["source_finding_ids"])
   472→    ]
   473→    for fp in stale_fps:
   474→        del dismissals[fp]
   475→    return len(stale_fps)
   476→
   477→
   478→__all__ = ["Concern", "cleanup_stale_dismissals", "generate_concerns"]
   479→
```

```
1→"""Mixed concerns detection (UI + data fetching + transforms in one file)."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import json
     6→import logging
     7→import re
     8→from pathlib import Path
     9→from typing import Any
    10→
    11→from desloppify.core._internal.text_utils import PROJECT_ROOT
    12→from desloppify.core.fallbacks import log_best_effort_failure
    13→from desloppify.core.discovery_api import find_tsx_files, rel
    14→from desloppify.core.output_api import colorize, print_table
    15→
    16→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    17→
    18→
    19→def detect_mixed_concerns(path: Path) -> tuple[list[dict[str, Any]], int]:
    20→    """Find files that mix UI rendering with data fetching, state management, and business logic.
    21→
    22→    Heuristic: a .tsx file that has both JSX returns AND direct API/supabase calls
    23→    or both UI components AND heavy data transformation.
    24→
    25→    Returns (entries, total_files_checked).
    26→    """
    27→    files = find_tsx_files(path)
    28→    entries = []
    29→    for filepath in files:
    30→        try:
    31→            p = (
    32→                Path(filepath)
    33→                if Path(filepath).is_absolute()
    34→                else PROJECT_ROOT / filepath
    35→            )
    36→            content = p.read_text()
    37→            loc = len(content.splitlines())
    38→            if loc < 100:
    39→                continue
    40→
    41→            concerns = []
    42→
    43→            # UI rendering
    44→            has_jsx = bool(re.search(r"return\s*\(?\s*<", content))
    45→            if has_jsx:
    46→                concerns.append("jsx_rendering")
    47→
    48→            # Data fetching
    49→            has_fetch = bool(
    50→                re.search(r"useQuery|useMutation|supabase\.|fetch\(|axios", content)
    51→            )
    52→            if has_fetch:
    53→                concerns.append("data_fetching")
    54→
    55→            # Direct supabase calls (should be in hooks/services)
    56→            has_supabase = bool(re.search(r"supabase\.\w+\.\w+\.\w+", content))
    57→            if has_supabase:
    58→                concerns.append("direct_supabase")
    59→
    60→            # Heavy data transformation
    61→            transform_patterns = len(
    62→                re.findall(r"\.(map|filter|reduce|sort|flatMap)\s*\(", content)
    63→            )
    64→            if transform_patterns >= 3:
    65→                concerns.append(f"data_transforms({transform_patterns})")
    66→
    67→            # Event handler definitions (>5 = probably doing too much)
    68→            handler_count = len(re.findall(r"(?:const|function)\s+handle\w+", content))
    69→            if handler_count >= 5:
    70→                concerns.append(f"handlers({handler_count})")
    71→
    72→            # Flag if 3+ concern types in one file
    73→            if len(concerns) >= 3:
    74→                entries.append(
    75→                    {
    76→                        "file": filepath,
    77→                        "loc": loc,
    78→                        "concerns": concerns,
    79→                        "concern_count": len(concerns),
    80→                    }
    81→                )
    82→        except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError) as exc:
    83→            log_best_effort_failure(
    84→                logger, f"read TSX concern candidate {filepath}", exc
    85→            )
    86→            continue
    87→    return sorted(entries, key=lambda e: -e["concern_count"]), len(files)
    88→
    89→
    90→def cmd_concerns(args: Any) -> None:
    91→    entries, _ = detect_mixed_concerns(Path(args.path))
    92→    if args.json:
    93→        print(json.dumps({"count": len(entries), "entries": entries}, indent=2))
    94→        return
    95→    if not entries:
    96→        print(colorize("No mixed-concern files found.", "green"))
    97→        return
    98→    print(colorize(f"\nMixed concerns: {len(entries)} files\n", "bold"))
    99→    rows = []
   100→    for e in entries[: args.top]:
   101→        rows.append([rel(e["file"]), str(e["loc"]), ", ".join(e["concerns"])])
   102→    print_table(["File", "LOC", "Concerns"], rows, [55, 5, 50])
   103→
```

```
494→def batch_concerns(
   495→    concerns: list,
   496→    *,
   497→    max_files: int | None = None,
   498→) -> dict | None:
   499→    """Build investigation batch from mechanical concern signals.
   500→
   501→    *concerns* should be a list of Concern dataclass instances from
   502→    ``desloppify.engine.concerns``.
   503→    """
   504→    if not concerns:
   505→        return None
   506→    types = sorted({c.type for c in concerns if c.type})
   507→    why_parts = ["mechanical detectors identified structural patterns needing judgment"]
   508→    if types:
   509→        why_parts.append(f"concern types: {', '.join(types)}")
   510→    files: list[str] = []
   511→    seen: set[str] = set()
   512→    for concern in concerns:
   513→        candidate = _normalize_file_path(getattr(concern, "file", ""))
   514→        if not candidate or candidate in seen:
   515→            continue
   516→        seen.add(candidate)
   517→        files.append(candidate)
   518→
   519→    total_candidate_files = len(files)
   520→    if (
   521→        max_files is not None
   522→        and isinstance(max_files, int)
   523→        and max_files > 0
   524→        and total_candidate_files > max_files
   525→    ):
   526→        files = files[:max_files]
   527→        why_parts.append(
   528→            f"truncated to {max_files} files from {total_candidate_files} candidates"
   529→        )
   530→
   531→    return {
   532→        "name": "Design coherence — Mechanical Concern Signals",
   533→        "dimensions": ["design_coherence"],
   534→        "files_to_read": files,
   535→        "why": "; ".join(why_parts),
   536→        "total_candidate_files": total_candidate_files,
   537→    }
   538→
   539→
   540→__all__ = ["batch_concerns", "build_investigation_batches", "filter_batches_to_dimensions"]
   541→
```

```
    97→def prepare_review(

   112→        if resolved_options.files is not None

   118→    already_cached = is_file_cache_enabled()

   130→                force_refresh=resolved_options.force_refresh,
   131→                files=all_files,
   132→            ),
   133→        )
   134→        file_requests = _build_file_requests(selected, lang, state)
   135→    finally:
   136→        if not already_cached:
   137→            disable_file_cache()
   138→

   180→def _build_file_requests(files: list[str], lang, state: dict) -> list[dict]:
   181→    """Build per-file review request dicts."""
   182→    file_requests = []
   183→    for filepath in files:
   184→        content = read_file_text(abs_path(filepath))
   185→        if content is None:
   186→            continue
   187→
   188→        rpath = rel(filepath)
   189→        zone = "production"
   190→        if lang.zone_map is not None:
   191→            zone = lang.zone_map.get(filepath).value
   192→
   193→        neighbors: dict
   194→        if lang.dep_graph:
   195→            entry = dep_graph_lookup(lang.dep_graph, filepath)
   196→            imports_raw = entry.get("imports", set())
   197→            importers_raw = entry.get("importers", set())
   198→            importer_count_value = importer_count(entry)
   199→            neighbors = {
   200→                "imports": _rel_list(imports_raw),
   201→                "importers": _rel_list(importers_raw),
   202→                "importer_count": importer_count_value,
   203→            }
   204→        else:
   205→            neighbors = {}
   206→
   207→        file_requests.append(
   208→            {
   209→                "file": rpath,
   210→                "content": content,
   211→                "zone": zone,
   212→                "loc": len(content.splitlines()),
   213→                "neighbors": neighbors,
   214→                "existing_findings": get_file_findings(state, filepath),
   215→            }
   216→        )
   217→    return file_requests
   218→
```

## Summary

I've successfully located all the concerns-related code and the holistic types module. Here are the key findings:

### 1. **Concern Class Definition**
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/concerns.py`
- **Line 21-31**: Main `Concern` dataclass definition
  - Fields: `type`, `file`, `summary`, `evidence`, `question`, `fingerprint`, `source_findings`
  - Frozen dataclass for immutability
- **Line 307-358**: `_file_concerns()` generator function - creates per-file design concerns from aggregated mechanical signals
- **Line 361-427**: `_cross_file_patterns()` generator function - identifies systemic patterns across 3+ files
- **Line 433-454**: `generate_concerns()` - main orchestrator that runs all generators and returns deduplicated concerns
- **Line 457-475**: `cleanup_stale_dismissals()` - removes outdated dismissals when source findings disappear

### 2. **batch_concerns Function**

```
    82→def _rel_list(s) -> list[str]:
    83→    """Normalize a set or list of paths to sorted relative paths (max 10)."""
    84→    if isinstance(s, set):
    85→        return sorted(rel(x) for x in s)[:10]
    86→    return [rel(x) for x in list(s)[:10]]

    89→def _normalize_max_files(value: Any) -> int | None:
    90→    """Normalize max_files input: None/<=0 means unlimited."""
    91→    if value in (None, ""):
    92→        return None
    93→    parsed = int(value)
    94→    return parsed if parsed > 0 else None

    97→def prepare_review(
    98→    path: Path,
    99→    lang: object,
   100→    state: dict,
   101→    options: ReviewPrepareOptions | None = None,
   102→) -> dict[str, object]:
   103→    """Prepare review data for agent consumption. Returns structured dict.
   104→
   105→    If *files* is provided, skip file_finder (avoids redundant filesystem walks
   106→    when the caller already has the file list, e.g. from _setup_lang).
   107→    """
   108→    resolved_options = options or ReviewPrepareOptions()
   109→    resolved_options.max_files = _normalize_max_files(resolved_options.max_files)
   110→    all_files = (
   111→        resolved_options.files
   112→        if resolved_options.files is not None
   113→        else (lang.file_finder(path) if lang.file_finder else [])
   114→    )
   115→
   116→    # Enable file cache for entire prepare operation — context building,
   117→    # file selection, and content extraction all read the same files.
   118→    already_cached = is_file_cache_enabled()
   119→    if not already_cached:
   120→        enable_file_cache()
   121→    try:
   122→        context = build_review_context(path, lang, state, files=all_files)
   123→        selected = select_files_for_review(
   124→            lang,
   125→            path,
   126→            state,
   127→            options=ReviewSelectionOptions(
   128→                max_files=resolved_options.max_files,
   129→                max_age_days=resolved_options.max_age_days,
   130→                force_refresh=resolved_options.force_refresh,
   131→                files=all_files,
   132→            ),
   133→        )
   134→        file_requests = _build_file_requests(selected, lang, state)
   135→    finally:
   136→        if not already_cached:
   137→            disable_file_cache()
   138→
   139→    default_dims, dimension_prompts, system_prompt = load_dimensions_for_lang(lang.name)
   140→    dims = resolve_dimensions(
   141→        cli_dimensions=resolved_options.dimensions,
   142→        config_dimensions=resolved_options.config_dimensions,
   143→        default_dimensions=default_dims,
   144→    )
   145→    lang_guide = get_lang_guidance(lang.name)
   146→    valid_dims = set(dimension_prompts)
   147→    invalid_requested = [
   148→        dim for dim in (resolved_options.dimensions or []) if dim not in valid_dims
   149→    ]
   150→    invalid_config = [
   151→        dim
   152→        for dim in (resolved_options.config_dimensions or [])
   153→        if dim not in valid_dims
   154→    ]
   155→
   156→    return {
   157→        "command": "review",
   158→        "language": lang.name,
   159→        "dimensions": dims,
   160→        "dimension_prompts": {
   161→            d: dimension_prompts[d] for d in dims if d in dimension_prompts
   162→        },
   163→        "lang_guidance": lang_guide,
   164→        "context": serialize_context(context),
   165→        "system_prompt": system_prompt,
   166→        "files": file_requests,
   167→        "total_candidates": len(file_requests),
   168→        "cache_status": {
   169→            "fresh": count_fresh(state, resolved_options.max_age_days),
   170→            "stale": count_stale(state, resolved_options.max_age_days),
   171→            "new": len(file_requests),
   172→        },
   173→        "invalid_dimensions": {
   174→            "requested": invalid_requested,
   175→            "config": invalid_config,
   176→        },
   177→    }

   180→def _build_file_requests(files: list[str], lang, state: dict) -> list[dict]:
   181→    """Build per-file review request dicts."""
   182→    file_requests = []
   183→    for filepath in files:
   184→        content = read_file_text(abs_path(filepath))
   185→        if content is None:
   186→            continue
   187→
   188→        rpath = rel(filepath)
   189→        zone = "production"
   190→        if lang.zone_map is not None:
   191→            zone = lang.zone_map.get(filepath).value
   192→
   193→        neighbors: dict
   194→        if lang.dep_graph:
   195→            entry = dep_graph_lookup(lang.dep_graph, filepath)
   196→            imports_raw = entry.get("imports", set())
   197→            importers_raw = entry.get("importers", set())
   198→            importer_count_value = importer_count(entry)
   199→            neighbors = {
   200→                "imports": _rel_list(imports_raw),
   201→                "importers": _rel_list(importers_raw),
   202→                "importer_count": importer_count_value,
   203→            }
   204→        else:
   205→            neighbors = {}
   206→
   207→        file_requests.append(
   208→            {
   209→                "file": rpath,
   210→                "content": content,
   211→                "zone": zone,
   212→                "loc": len(content.splitlines()),
   213→                "neighbors": neighbors,
   214→                "existing_findings": get_file_findings(state, filepath),
   215→            }
   216→        )
   217→    return file_requests
   218→
   219→
   220→def prepare_holistic_review(
   221→    path: Path,
   222→    lang: object,
   223→    state: dict,
   224→    options: HolisticReviewPrepareOptions | None = None,
   225→) -> dict[str, object]:
   226→    """Prepare holistic review data for agent consumption. Returns structured dict."""
   227→    resolved_options = options or HolisticReviewPrepareOptions()
   228→    all_files = (
   229→        resolved_options.files
   230→        if resolved_options.files is not None
   231→        else (lang.file_finder(path) if lang.file_finder else [])
   232→    )
   233→
   234→    already_cached = is_file_cache_enabled()
   235→    if not already_cached:
   236→        enable_file_cache()
   237→    try:
   238→        context = HolisticContext.from_raw(
   239→            build_holistic_context(path, lang, state, files=all_files)
   240→        )
   241→        # Also include per-file review context for reference
   242→        review_ctx = build_review_context(path, lang, state, files=all_files)
   243→    finally:
   244→        if not already_cached:
   245→            disable_file_cache()
   246→
   247→    default_dims, holistic_prompts, system_prompt = load_dimensions_for_lang(lang.name)
   248→    _, per_file_prompts, _ = load_dimensions_for_lang(lang.name)
   249→    dims = resolve_dimensions(
   250→        cli_dimensions=resolved_options.dimensions,
   251→        lang_name=lang.name,
   252→        default_dimensions=default_dims,
   253→    )
   254→    lang_guide = get_lang_guidance(lang.name)
   255→    valid_dims = set(holistic_prompts) | set(per_file_prompts)
   256→    invalid_requested = [
   257→        dim for dim in (resolved_options.dimensions or []) if dim not in valid_dims
   258→    ]
   259→    invalid_default = [dim for dim in default_dims if dim not in valid_dims]
   260→    batches = _build_investigation_batches(
   261→        context,
   262→        lang,
   263→        repo_root=path,
   264→        max_files_per_batch=resolved_options.max_files_per_batch,
   265→    )
   266→
   267→    # Append design-coherence batch from mechanical concern signals.
   268→    try:
   269→        from desloppify.engine.concerns import generate_concerns
   270→
   271→        concerns = generate_concerns(state, lang_name=lang.name)
   272→        concerns_batch = _batch_concerns(
   273→            concerns,
   274→            max_files=resolved_options.max_files_per_batch,
   275→        )
   276→        if concerns_batch:
   277→            batches.append(concerns_batch)
   278→    except (ImportError, AttributeError, TypeError, ValueError) as exc:
   279→        logger.debug("Concern generation failed (best-effort): %s", exc)
   280→
   281→    batches = _filter_batches_to_dimensions(
   282→        batches,
   283→        dims,
   284→        fallback_max_files=resolved_options.max_files_per_batch,
   285→    )
   286→    include_full_sweep = bool(resolved_options.include_full_sweep)
   287→    # Explicitly scoped dimension runs should stay scoped by default.
   288→    if resolved_options.dimensions:
   289→        include_full_sweep = False
   290→    if include_full_sweep:
   291→        append_full_sweep_batch(
   292→            batches=batches,
   293→            dims=dims,
   294→            all_files=all_files,
   295→            lang=lang,
   296→            max_files=resolved_options.max_files_per_batch,
   297→        )
   298→
   299→    # Holistic mode can receive per-file-oriented dimensions via CLI suggestions.
   300→    # Attach whichever prompt definition exists so reviewers always get guidance.
   301→    selected_prompts: dict[str, dict[str, object]] = {}
   302→    for dim in dims:
   303→        prompt = holistic_prompts.get(dim)
   304→        if prompt is None:
   305→            prompt = per_file_prompts.get(dim)
   306→        if prompt is None:
   307→            continue
   308→        selected_prompts[dim] = prompt
   309→
   310→    payload = {
   311→        "command": "review",
   312→        "mode": "holistic",
   313→        "language": lang.name,
   314→        "dimensions": dims,
   315→        "dimension_prompts": selected_prompts,
   316→        "lang_guidance": lang_guide,
   317→        "holistic_context": context.to_dict(),
   318→        "review_context": serialize_context(review_ctx),
   319→        "system_prompt": system_prompt,
   320→        "total_files": context.codebase_stats.get("total_files", 0),
   321→        "workflow": HOLISTIC_WORKFLOW,
   322→        "investigation_batches": batches,
   323→        "invalid_dimensions": {
   324→            "requested": invalid_requested,

   328→    if resolved_options.include_issue_history:
```

```
    15→def _render_scan_evidence_focus(dim_set: set[str]) -> str:
    16→    """Render dimension-specific scan_evidence guidance."""
    17→    parts: list[str] = []
    18→    if "initialization_coupling" in dim_set:
    19→        parts.append(
    20→            "9e. For initialization_coupling, use evidence from "
    21→            "`holistic_context.scan_evidence.mutable_globals` and "
    22→            "`holistic_context.errors.mutable_globals`. Investigate initialization ordering "
    23→            "dependencies, coupling through shared mutable state, and whether state should "
    24→            "be encapsulated behind a proper registry/context manager.\n"
    25→        )
    26→    if "design_coherence" in dim_set:
    27→        parts.append(
    28→            "9f. For design_coherence, use evidence from "
    29→            "`holistic_context.scan_evidence.signal_density` — files where "
    30→            "multiple mechanical detectors fired. Investigate what design change would address "
    31→            "multiple signals simultaneously. Check `scan_evidence.complexity_hotspots` for "
    32→            "files with high responsibility cluster counts.\n"
    33→        )
    34→    if "error_consistency" in dim_set:
    35→        parts.append(
    36→            "9g. For error_consistency, use evidence from "
    37→            "`holistic_context.errors.exception_hotspots` — files with "
    38→            "concentrated exception handling findings. Investigate whether error handling is "
    39→            "designed or accidental. Check for broad catches masking specific failure modes.\n"
    40→        )
    41→    if "cross_module_architecture" in dim_set:
    42→        parts.append(
    43→            "9h. For cross_module_architecture, also consult "
    44→            "`holistic_context.coupling.boundary_violations` for import paths that "
    45→            "cross architectural boundaries, and `holistic_context.dependencies.deferred_import_density` "
    46→            "for files with many function-level imports (proxy for cycle pressure).\n"
    47→        )
    48→    if "convention_outlier" in dim_set:
    49→        parts.append(
    50→            "9i. For convention_outlier, also consult "
    51→            "`holistic_context.conventions.duplicate_clusters` for cross-file "
    52→            "function duplication and `conventions.naming_drift` for directory-level naming "
    53→            "inconsistency.\n"
    54→        )
    55→    return "".join(parts)
    56→
    57→
    58→def _render_historical_focus(batch: dict[str, object]) -> str:
    59→    focus = batch.get("historical_issue_focus")
    60→    if not isinstance(focus, dict):
    61→        return ""
    62→
    63→    selected_raw = focus.get("selected_count", 0)
    64→    try:
    65→        selected_count = max(0, int(selected_raw))
    66→    except (TypeError, ValueError):
    67→        selected_count = 0
    68→
    69→    issues = focus.get("issues", [])
    70→    if not isinstance(issues, list):
    71→        issues = []
    72→
    73→    if selected_count <= 0 or not issues:
    74→        return ""
    75→
    76→    lines: list[str] = []
    77→    lines.append(
    78→        "Previously flagged issues (from past reviews of these dimensions):"
    79→    )
    80→    lines.append(
    81→        "Check whether each issue still exists in the current code. Do not re-report"
    82→        " issues that have been fixed or marked wontfix — focus on what remains or"
    83→        " what is new. If several past issues share a root cause, call that out."
    84→    )
    85→
    86→    for entry in issues:
    87→        if not isinstance(entry, dict):
    88→            continue
    89→        status = str(entry.get("status", "")).strip()
    90→        summary = str(entry.get("summary", "")).strip()
    91→        note = str(entry.get("note", "")).strip()
    92→
    93→        line = f"  - [{status}] {summary}"
    94→        if note:
    95→            line += f" (note: {note})"
    96→        lines.append(line)
    97→    return "\n".join(lines) + "\n\n"

   100→def render_batch_prompt(
   101→    *,
   102→    repo_root: Path,
   103→    packet_path: Path,
   104→    batch_index: int,
   105→    batch: dict[str, object],
   106→) -> str:
   107→    """Render one subagent prompt for a holistic investigation batch."""
   108→    name = str(batch.get("name", f"Batch {batch_index + 1}"))
   109→    dims_raw = batch.get("dimensions", [])
   110→    dims = (
   111→        [str(d) for d in dims_raw if isinstance(d, str) and d]
   112→        if isinstance(dims_raw, list | tuple)
   113→        else []
   114→    )
   115→    why = str(batch.get("why", "")).strip()
   116→    files_raw = batch.get("files_to_read", [])
   117→    files = (
   118→        [str(f) for f in files_raw if isinstance(f, str) and f]
   119→        if isinstance(files_raw, list | tuple)
   120→        else []
   121→    )
   122→    findings_cap = max_batch_findings_for_dimension_count(len(dims))
   123→    file_lines = "\n".join(f"- {f}" for f in files) if files else "- (none)"
   124→    dim_text = ", ".join(dims) if dims else "(none)"
   125→    historical_focus = _render_historical_focus(batch)
   126→    dim_set = set(dims)
   127→    package_org_focus = ""
   128→    if "package_organization" in dim_set:
   129→        package_org_focus = (
   130→            "9a. For package_organization, ground scoring in objective structure signals from "
   131→            "`holistic_context.structure` (root_files fan_in/fan_out roles, directory_profiles, "
   132→            "coupling_matrix). Prefer thresholded evidence (for example: fan_in < 5 for root "
   133→            "stragglers, import-affinity > 60%, directories > 10 files with mixed concerns).\n"
   134→            "9b. Suggestions must include a staged reorg plan (target folders, move order, "
   135→            "and import-update/validation commands).\n"
   136→            "9c. Also consult `holistic_context.structure.flat_dir_findings` for directories "
   137→            "flagged as overloaded, fragmented, or thin-wrapper patterns.\n"
   138→        )
   139→    abstraction_focus = ""
   140→    if "abstraction_fitness" in dim_set:
   141→        abstraction_focus = (
   142→            "9d. For abstraction_fitness, use evidence from `holistic_context.abstractions`:\n"
   143→            "  - `delegation_heavy_classes`: classes where most methods forward to an inner "
   144→            "object — entries include class_name, delegate_target, sample_methods, and line number.\n"
   145→            "  - `facade_modules`: re-export-only modules with high re_export_ratio — entries "
   146→            "include samples (re-exported names) and loc.\n"
   147→            "  - `typed_dict_violations`: TypedDict fields accessed via .get()/.setdefault()/.pop() "
   148→            "— entries include typed_dict_name, violation_type, field, and line number.\n"
   149→            "  - `complexity_hotspots`: files where mechanical analysis found extreme parameter "
   150→            "counts, deep nesting, or disconnected responsibility clusters.\n"
   151→            "  Include `delegation_density`, `definition_directness`, and `type_discipline` "
   152→            "alongside existing sub-axes in dimension_notes when evidence supports it.\n"
   153→        )
   154→    scan_evidence_focus = _render_scan_evidence_focus(dim_set)
   155→
   156→    return (
   157→        "You are a focused subagent reviewer for a single holistic investigation batch.\n\n"
   158→        f"Repository root: {repo_root}\n"

   165→        f"{file_lines}\n\n"
   166→        f"{historical_focus}"
   167→        "Task requirements:\n"
   168→        "1. Read the blind packet and follow `system_prompt` constraints exactly.\n"

   171→        "    wontfix issues. Use them as starting points to look deeper — inspect adjacent code\n"
   172→        "    and related modules for defects the prior review may have missed.\n"
   173→        "1c. Think structurally: when you spot multiple individual issues that share a common\n"
   174→        "    root cause (missing abstraction, duplicated pattern, inconsistent convention),\n"
   175→        "    explain the deeper structural issue in the finding, not just the surface symptom.\n"
   176→        "    If the pattern is significant enough, report the structural issue as its own finding\n"
   177→        "    with appropriate fix_scope ('multi_file_refactor' or 'architectural_change') and\n"
   178→        "    use `root_cause_cluster` to connect related symptom findings together.\n"
   179→        "2. Start with the seed files, then freely explore additional repository files likely to surface material issues.\n"
   180→        "2a. Prioritize high-signal leads: unexplored/lightly reviewed files, historical issue areas, and hotspot neighbors "
   181→        "(high coupling, god modules, large files, churn seams).\n"
   182→        "2b. Keep exploration targeted — follow strongest evidence paths first instead of attempting exhaustive coverage.\n"
   183→        "2c. Keep findings and scoring scoped to this batch's listed dimensions.\n"
   184→        "2d. Respect scope controls in the blind packet config: do not include files/directories marked by "
   185→        "`exclude`, `ignore`, or zone overrides that classify files as non-production (test/config/generated/vendor).\n"
   186→        f"3. Return 0-{findings_cap} high-quality findings for this batch (empty array allowed).\n"

   188→        "3b. Do not default to 100. Reserve 100 for genuinely exemplary evidence in this batch.\n"
```

```
1→"""Context building for review: ReviewContext, shared helpers, heuristic signals."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import re
     6→from collections import Counter
     7→from pathlib import Path
     8→
     9→from desloppify.core.discovery_api import (
    10→    disable_file_cache,
    11→    enable_file_cache,
    12→    is_file_cache_enabled,
    13→    read_file_text,
    14→    rel,
    15→    resolve_path,
    16→)
    17→from desloppify.intelligence.review._context.models import ReviewContext
    18→from desloppify.intelligence.review._context.patterns import (
    19→    CLASS_NAME_RE,
    20→    ERROR_PATTERNS,
    21→    FUNC_NAME_RE,
    22→    NAME_PREFIX_RE,
    23→    default_review_module_patterns,
    24→)
    25→from desloppify.intelligence.review.context_signals.ai import gather_ai_debt_signals
    26→from desloppify.intelligence.review.context_signals.auth import gather_auth_context
    27→from desloppify.intelligence.review.context_signals.migration import (
    28→    classify_error_strategy,
    29→)
    30→
    31→# ── Shared helpers ────────────────────────────────────────────────
    32→
    33→
    34→def abs_path(filepath: str) -> str:
    35→    """Resolve filepath to absolute using resolve_path."""
    36→    return resolve_path(filepath)
    37→
    38→
    39→def file_excerpt(filepath: str, max_lines: int = 30) -> str | None:
    40→    """Read first *max_lines* of a file, returning the text or None."""
    41→    content = read_file_text(abs_path(filepath))
    42→    if content is None:
    43→        return None
    44→    lines = content.splitlines(keepends=True)
    45→    if len(lines) <= max_lines:
    46→        return content
    47→    return "".join(lines[:max_lines]) + f"\n... ({len(lines) - max_lines} more lines)"
    48→
    49→
    50→def dep_graph_lookup(graph: dict, filepath: str) -> dict:
    51→    """Look up a file in the dep graph, trying absolute and relative keys."""
    52→    resolved = resolve_path(filepath)
    53→    entry = graph.get(resolved)
    54→    if entry is not None:
    55→        return entry
    56→    # Try relative path
    57→    rpath = rel(filepath)
    58→    entry = graph.get(rpath)
    59→    if entry is not None:
    60→        return entry
    61→    return {}
    62→
    63→
    64→def importer_count(entry: dict) -> int:
    65→    """Extract importer count from a dep graph entry."""
    66→    importers = entry.get("importers", set())
    67→    if isinstance(importers, set):
    68→        return len(importers)
    69→    return entry.get("importer_count", 0)
    70→
    71→
    72→# ── Per-file review context builder ──────────────────────────────
    73→
    74→
    75→def build_review_context(
    76→    path: Path,
    77→    lang,
    78→    state: dict,
    79→    files: list[str] | None = None,
    80→) -> ReviewContext:
    81→    """Gather codebase conventions for contextual evaluation.
    82→
    83→    If *files* is provided, skip file_finder (avoids redundant filesystem walks).
    84→    """
    85→    if files is None:
    86→        files = lang.file_finder(path) if lang.file_finder else []
    87→    ctx = ReviewContext()
    88→
    89→    if not files:
    90→        return ctx
    91→
    92→    already_cached = is_file_cache_enabled()
    93→    if not already_cached:
    94→        enable_file_cache()
    95→    try:
    96→        return _build_review_context_inner(files, lang, state, ctx)
    97→    finally:
    98→        if not already_cached:
    99→            disable_file_cache()
   100→
   101→
   102→def _build_review_context_inner(
   103→    files: list[str],
   104→    lang,
   105→    state: dict,
   106→    ctx: ReviewContext,
   107→) -> ReviewContext:
   108→    """Inner context builder (runs with file cache enabled)."""
   109→    # Pre-read all file contents once (cache will store them)
   110→    file_contents: dict[str, str] = {}
   111→    for filepath in files:
   112→        content = read_file_text(abs_path(filepath))
   113→        if content is not None:
   114→            file_contents[filepath] = content
   115→
   116→    # 1. Naming vocabulary — extract function/class names, count prefixes
   117→    prefix_counter: Counter = Counter()
   118→    total_names = 0
   119→    for content in file_contents.values():
   120→        for name in FUNC_NAME_RE.findall(content) + CLASS_NAME_RE.findall(content):
   121→            total_names += 1
   122→            match = NAME_PREFIX_RE.match(name)
   123→            if match:
   124→                prefix_counter[match.group(1)] += 1
   125→    ctx.naming_vocabulary = {
   126→        "prefixes": dict(prefix_counter.most_common(20)),
   127→        "total_names": total_names,
   128→    }
   129→
   130→    # 2. Error handling conventions — scan for patterns
   131→    error_counts: Counter = Counter()
   132→    for content in file_contents.values():
   133→        for pattern_name, pattern in ERROR_PATTERNS.items():
   134→            if pattern.search(content):
   135→                error_counts[pattern_name] += 1
   136→    ctx.error_conventions = dict(error_counts)
   137→
   138→    # 3. Module patterns — what each directory typically uses
   139→    dir_patterns: dict[str, Counter] = {}
   140→    module_pattern_fn = getattr(lang, "review_module_patterns_fn", None)
   141→    if not callable(module_pattern_fn):
   142→        module_pattern_fn = default_review_module_patterns
   143→    for filepath, content in file_contents.items():
   144→        parts = Path(filepath).parts
   145→        if len(parts) < 2:
   146→            continue
   147→        dir_name = parts[-2] + "/"
   148→        counter = dir_patterns.setdefault(dir_name, Counter())
   149→        pattern_names = module_pattern_fn(content)
   150→        if not isinstance(pattern_names, list | tuple | set):
   151→            pattern_names = default_review_module_patterns(content)
   152→        for pattern_name in pattern_names:
   153→            counter[pattern_name] += 1
   154→        if re.search(r"\bclass\s+\w+", content):
   155→            counter["class_based"] += 1
   156→    ctx.module_patterns = {
   157→        d: dict(c.most_common(3))
   158→        for d, c in dir_patterns.items()
   159→        if sum(c.values()) >= 3
   160→    }
   161→
   162→    # 4. Import graph summary — top files by importer count
   163→    if lang.dep_graph:
   164→        graph = lang.dep_graph
   165→        importer_counts = {}
   166→        for filepath, entry in graph.items():
   167→            count = importer_count(entry)
   168→            if count > 0:
   169→                importer_counts[rel(filepath)] = count
   170→        top = sorted(importer_counts.items(), key=lambda item: -item[1])[:20]
   171→        ctx.import_graph_summary = {"top_imported": dict(top)}
   172→
   173→    # 5. Zone distribution
   174→    if lang.zone_map is not None:
   175→        ctx.zone_distribution = lang.zone_map.counts()
   176→
   177→    # 6. Existing findings per file (summaries only)
   178→    findings = state.get("findings", {})
   179→    by_file: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
   180→    for finding in findings.values():
   181→        if finding["status"] == "open":
   182→            by_file.setdefault(finding["file"], []).append(
   183→                f"{finding['detector']}: {finding['summary'][:80]}"
   184→            )
   185→    ctx.existing_findings = by_file
   186→
   187→    # 7. Codebase stats
   188→    total_files = len(file_contents)
   189→    total_loc = sum(len(content.splitlines()) for content in file_contents.values())
   190→    ctx.codebase_stats = {
   191→        "total_files": total_files,
   192→        "total_loc": total_loc,
   193→        "avg_file_loc": total_loc // total_files if total_files else 0,
   194→    }
   195→    _ = (
   196→        ctx.codebase_stats["total_files"],
   197→        ctx.codebase_stats["total_loc"],
   198→        ctx.codebase_stats["avg_file_loc"],
   199→    )
   200→
   201→    # 8. Sibling function conventions — what naming/patterns neighbors in same dir use
   202→    dir_functions: dict[str, Counter] = {}
   203→    for filepath, content in file_contents.items():
   204→        parts = Path(filepath).parts
   205→        if len(parts) < 2:
   206→            continue
   207→        dir_name = parts[-2] + "/"
   208→        counter = dir_functions.setdefault(dir_name, Counter())
   209→        for name in FUNC_NAME_RE.findall(content):
   210→            match = NAME_PREFIX_RE.match(name)
   211→            if match:
   212→                counter[match.group(1)] += 1
   213→    ctx.sibling_conventions = {
   214→        d: dict(c.most_common(5))
   215→        for d, c in dir_functions.items()
   216→        if sum(c.values()) >= 3
   217→    }
   218→
   219→    # 9. AI debt signals
   220→    ctx.ai_debt_signals = gather_ai_debt_signals(file_contents, rel_fn=rel)
   221→
   222→    # 10. Auth patterns
   223→    ctx.auth_patterns = gather_auth_context(file_contents, rel_fn=rel)
   224→
   225→    # 11. Error strategies per file
   226→    strategies: dict[str, str] = {}
   227→    for filepath, content in file_contents.items():
   228→        strategy = classify_error_strategy(content)
   229→        if strategy:
   230→            strategies[rel(filepath)] = strategy
   231→    ctx.error_strategies = strategies
   232→
   233→    ctx.normalize_sections(strict=True)
   234→    return ctx
   235→
   236→
   237→def serialize_context(ctx: ReviewContext) -> dict:
   238→    """Convert ReviewContext to a JSON-serializable dict."""
   239→    def _section_dict(value: object) -> dict:
   240→        if hasattr(value, "to_dict") and callable(value.to_dict):
   241→            data = value.to_dict()
   242→            return data if isinstance(data, dict) else {}
   243→        return dict(value) if isinstance(value, dict) else {}
   244→
   245→    metrics = ("total_files", "total_loc", "avg_file_loc")
   246→    codebase_stats = _section_dict(ctx.codebase_stats)
   247→    out = {
   248→        "naming_vocabulary": _section_dict(ctx.naming_vocabulary),
   249→        "error_conventions": _section_dict(ctx.error_conventions),
   250→        "module_patterns": _section_dict(ctx.module_patterns),
   251→        "import_graph_summary": _section_dict(ctx.import_graph_summary),
   252→        "zone_distribution": _section_dict(ctx.zone_distribution),
   253→        "existing_findings": _section_dict(ctx.existing_findings),
   254→        "codebase_stats": {
   255→            key: int(codebase_stats.get(key, 0))
   256→            for key in metrics
   257→        },
   258→        "sibling_conventions": _section_dict(ctx.sibling_conventions),
   259→    }
   260→    if ctx.ai_debt_signals:
   261→        out["ai_debt_signals"] = _section_dict(ctx.ai_debt_signals)
   262→    if ctx.auth_patterns:
   263→        out["auth_patterns"] = _section_dict(ctx.auth_patterns)
   264→    if ctx.error_strategies:
   265→        out["error_strategies"] = _section_dict(ctx.error_strategies)
   266→    return out
   267→
```

```
1→"""Data models for review-context construction."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from collections.abc import Iterator, Mapping, MutableMapping
     6→from dataclasses import dataclass, field
     7→from typing import Any
     8→
     9→
    10→class ReviewContextSchemaError(ValueError):
    11→    """Raised when contextual review payloads violate section-shape contracts."""
    12→
    13→
    14→@dataclass
    15→class SectionPayload(MutableMapping[str, Any]):
    16→    """Named mapping wrapper for one review-context section."""
    17→
    18→    name: str
    19→    _data: dict[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)
    20→
    21→    def __getitem__(self, key: str) -> Any:
    22→        return self._data[key]
    23→
    24→    def __setitem__(self, key: str, value: Any) -> None:
    25→        self._data[key] = value
    26→
    27→    def __delitem__(self, key: str) -> None:
    28→        del self._data[key]
    29→
    30→    def __iter__(self) -> Iterator[str]:
    31→        return iter(self._data)
    32→
    33→    def __len__(self) -> int:
    34→        return len(self._data)
    35→
    36→    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
    37→        return dict(self._data)
    38→
    39→    def __eq__(self, other: object) -> bool:
    40→        if isinstance(other, Mapping):
    41→            return self._data == dict(other)
    42→        return False
    43→
    44→
    45→def _empty_section(name: str) -> SectionPayload:
    46→    return SectionPayload(name=name)
    47→
    48→
    49→def _coerce_section(
    50→    *,
    51→    section: str,
    52→    value: object,
    53→    strict: bool,
    54→) -> SectionPayload:
    55→    if isinstance(value, SectionPayload):
    56→        if value.name == section:
    57→            return value
    58→        return SectionPayload(name=section, _data=value.to_dict())
    59→    if isinstance(value, Mapping):
    60→        return SectionPayload(name=section, _data=dict(value))
    61→    if value is None:
    62→        return SectionPayload(name=section)
    63→    if strict:
    64→        raise ReviewContextSchemaError(
    65→            f"review context section '{section}' must be an object, got {type(value).__name__}"
    66→        )
    67→    return SectionPayload(name=section)
    68→
    69→
    70→@dataclass
    71→class ReviewContext:
    72→    """Codebase-wide context for contextual file evaluation."""
    73→
    74→    naming_vocabulary: SectionPayload = field(
    75→        default_factory=lambda: _empty_section("naming_vocabulary")
    76→    )
    77→    error_conventions: SectionPayload = field(
    78→        default_factory=lambda: _empty_section("error_conventions")
    79→    )
    80→    module_patterns: SectionPayload = field(
    81→        default_factory=lambda: _empty_section("module_patterns")
    82→    )
    83→    import_graph_summary: SectionPayload = field(
    84→        default_factory=lambda: _empty_section("import_graph_summary")
    85→    )
    86→    zone_distribution: SectionPayload = field(
    87→        default_factory=lambda: _empty_section("zone_distribution")
    88→    )
    89→    existing_findings: SectionPayload = field(
    90→        default_factory=lambda: _empty_section("existing_findings")
    91→    )
    92→    codebase_stats: SectionPayload = field(
    93→        default_factory=lambda: _empty_section("codebase_stats")
    94→    )
    95→    sibling_conventions: SectionPayload = field(
    96→        default_factory=lambda: _empty_section("sibling_conventions")
    97→    )
    98→    ai_debt_signals: SectionPayload = field(
    99→        default_factory=lambda: _empty_section("ai_debt_signals")
   100→    )
   101→    auth_patterns: SectionPayload = field(
   102→        default_factory=lambda: _empty_section("auth_patterns")
   103→    )
   104→    error_strategies: SectionPayload = field(
   105→        default_factory=lambda: _empty_section("error_strategies")
   106→    )
   107→
   108→    _SECTION_NAMES = (
   109→        "naming_vocabulary",
   110→        "error_conventions",
   111→        "module_patterns",
   112→        "import_graph_summary",
   113→        "zone_distribution",
   114→        "existing_findings",
   115→        "codebase_stats",
   116→        "sibling_conventions",
   117→        "ai_debt_signals",
   118→        "auth_patterns",
   119→        "error_strategies",
   120→    )
   121→
   122→    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
   123→        self.normalize_sections(strict=True)
   124→
   125→    def normalize_sections(self, *, strict: bool) -> None:
   126→        for section in self._SECTION_NAMES:
   127→            setattr(
   128→                self,
   129→                section,
   130→                _coerce_section(section=section, value=getattr(self, section), strict=strict),
   131→            )
   132→
   133→
   134→@dataclass
   135→class HolisticContext:
   136→    """Typed seam contract for holistic review context pipelines."""
   137→
   138→    architecture: SectionPayload = field(
   139→        default_factory=lambda: _empty_section("architecture")
   140→    )
   141→    coupling: SectionPayload = field(default_factory=lambda: _empty_section("coupling"))
   142→    conventions: SectionPayload = field(
   143→        default_factory=lambda: _empty_section("conventions")
   144→    )
   145→    errors: SectionPayload = field(default_factory=lambda: _empty_section("errors"))
   146→    abstractions: SectionPayload = field(
   147→        default_factory=lambda: _empty_section("abstractions")
   148→    )
   149→    dependencies: SectionPayload = field(
   150→        default_factory=lambda: _empty_section("dependencies")
   151→    )
   152→    testing: SectionPayload = field(default_factory=lambda: _empty_section("testing"))
   153→    api_surface: SectionPayload = field(
   154→        default_factory=lambda: _empty_section("api_surface")
   155→    )
   156→    structure: SectionPayload = field(default_factory=lambda: _empty_section("structure"))
   157→    codebase_stats: SectionPayload = field(
   158→        default_factory=lambda: _empty_section("codebase_stats")
   159→    )
   160→    authorization: SectionPayload = field(
   161→        default_factory=lambda: _empty_section("authorization")
   162→    )
   163→    ai_debt_signals: SectionPayload = field(
   164→        default_factory=lambda: _empty_section("ai_debt_signals")
   165→    )
   166→    migration_signals: SectionPayload = field(
   167→        default_factory=lambda: _empty_section("migration_signals")
   168→    )
   169→    scan_evidence: SectionPayload = field(
   170→        default_factory=lambda: _empty_section("scan_evidence")
   171→    )
   172→
   173→    _SECTION_NAMES = (
   174→        "architecture",
   175→        "coupling",
   176→        "conventions",
   177→        "errors",
   178→        "abstractions",
   179→        "dependencies",
   180→        "testing",
   181→        "api_surface",
   182→        "structure",
   183→        "codebase_stats",
   184→        "authorization",
   185→        "ai_debt_signals",
   186→        "migration_signals",
   187→        "scan_evidence",
   188→    )
   189→
   190→    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
   191→        self.normalize_sections(strict=True)
   192→
   193→    def normalize_sections(self, *, strict: bool) -> None:
   194→        for section in self._SECTION_NAMES:
   195→            setattr(
   196→                self,
   197→                section,
   198→                _coerce_section(section=section, value=getattr(self, section), strict=strict),
   199→            )
   200→
   201→    @classmethod
   202→    def from_raw(cls, payload: Mapping[str, Any] | None) -> HolisticContext:
   203→        if payload is not None and not isinstance(payload, Mapping):
   204→            raise ReviewContextSchemaError(
   205→                f"holistic review context payload must be an object, got {type(payload).__name__}"
   206→            )
   207→        raw = payload if isinstance(payload, Mapping) else {}
   208→        return cls(**{section: raw.get(section) for section in cls._SECTION_NAMES})
   209→
   210→    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, object]:
   211→        return {section: getattr(self, section).to_dict() for section in self._SECTION_NAMES}
   212→
   213→
   214→__all__ = [
   215→    "HolisticContext",
   216→    "ReviewContext",
   217→    "ReviewContextSchemaError",
   218→    "SectionPayload",
   219→]
   220→
```

```
1→"""Load and validate review dimension payloads from JSON data files."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import copy
     6→import json
     7→from functools import lru_cache
     8→from pathlib import Path
     9→
    10→from desloppify.intelligence.review.feedback_contract import ensure_prompt_contract
    11→from desloppify.intelligence.review.dimensions.validation import (
    12→    parse_dimensions_payload,
    13→)
    14→
    15→_LANG_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[3] / "languages"
    16→_LANG_DATA_SUBDIR = "review_data"
    17→_DATA_DIR = _LANG_DIR / "_framework" / _LANG_DATA_SUBDIR
    18→
    19→# Canonical filename for the unified dimensions payload.
    20→_DIMENSIONS_FILE = "dimensions.json"
    21→
    22→
    23→def _load_json_payload_from_path(path: Path) -> dict:
    24→    """Load a JSON payload from *path* and return a dict."""
    25→    try:
    26→        raw = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    27→    except OSError as exc:
    28→        raise RuntimeError(f"Unable to read dimensions payload: {path}") from exc
    29→
    30→    try:
    31→        payload = json.loads(raw)
    32→    except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
    33→        raise ValueError(f"Invalid JSON in dimensions payload: {path}") from exc
    34→
    35→    if not isinstance(payload, dict):
    36→        raise ValueError(f"Dimensions payload must be a JSON object: {path}")
    37→    return payload
    38→
    39→
    40→def _lang_payload_path(lang_name: str, filename: str) -> Path:
    41→    """Resolve the per-language review payload path."""
    42→    return _LANG_DIR / lang_name / _LANG_DATA_SUBDIR / filename
    43→
    44→
    45→def _override_filename(filename: str) -> str:
    46→    """Convert ``foo.json`` to ``foo.override.json``."""
    47→    if not filename.endswith(".json"):
    48→        return f"{filename}.override.json"
    49→    return f"{filename[:-5]}.override.json"
    50→
    51→
    52→def _load_json_payload(filename: str) -> dict:
    53→    """Load a JSON payload from the shared language review-data directory."""
    54→    return _load_json_payload_from_path(_DATA_DIR / filename)
    55→
    56→
    57→def _validate_optional_string_list(value: object, *, context: str) -> list[str]:
    58→    """Validate an optional list of strings (empty allowed)."""
    59→    if value is None:
    60→        return []
    61→    if not isinstance(value, list):
    62→        raise ValueError(f"{context} must be a list of strings")
    63→    out: list[str] = []
    64→    for idx, item in enumerate(value):
    65→        if not isinstance(item, str) or not item.strip():
    66→            raise ValueError(f"{context}[{idx}] must be a non-empty string")
    67→        out.append(item)
    68→    return out
    69→
    70→
    71→def _apply_dimensions_override(
    72→    base_payload: dict,
    73→    override_payload: dict,
    74→    *,
    75→    dims_key: str,
    76→    context: str,
    77→) -> dict:
    78→    """Apply a language override payload to a base dimensions payload."""
    79→    if not isinstance(override_payload, dict):
    80→        raise ValueError(f"{context} must be a JSON object")
    81→
    82→    allowed = {
    83→        dims_key,
    84→        f"{dims_key}_append",
    85→        f"{dims_key}_remove",
    86→        "dimension_prompts",
    87→        "dimension_prompts_remove",
    88→        "system_prompt",
    89→        "system_prompt_append",
    90→    }
    91→    actual = set(override_payload)
    92→    extra = sorted(actual - allowed)
    93→    if extra:
    94→        raise ValueError(f"{context} has unsupported keys: {extra}")
    95→
    96→    out = copy.deepcopy(base_payload)
    97→
    98→    if dims_key in override_payload:
    99→        out[dims_key] = override_payload[dims_key]
   100→
   101→    dims = list(out.get(dims_key, []))
   102→    for dim in _validate_optional_string_list(
   103→        override_payload.get(f"{dims_key}_append"),
   104→        context=f"{context}.{dims_key}_append",
   105→    ):
   106→        if dim not in dims:
   107→            dims.append(dim)
   108→
   109→    remove_dims = set(
   110→        _validate_optional_string_list(
   111→            override_payload.get(f"{dims_key}_remove"),
   112→            context=f"{context}.{dims_key}_remove",
   113→        )
   114→    )
   115→    if remove_dims:
   116→        dims = [dim for dim in dims if dim not in remove_dims]
   117→    out[dims_key] = dims
   118→
   119→    if "dimension_prompts" in override_payload:
   120→        prompt_overrides = override_payload["dimension_prompts"]
   121→        if not isinstance(prompt_overrides, dict):
   122→            raise ValueError(f"{context}.dimension_prompts must be an object")
   123→        prompts = dict(out.get("dimension_prompts", {}))
   124→        for dim_name, prompt in prompt_overrides.items():
   125→            prompts[dim_name] = prompt
   126→        out["dimension_prompts"] = prompts
   127→
   128→    remove_prompts = _validate_optional_string_list(
   129→        override_payload.get("dimension_prompts_remove"),
   130→        context=f"{context}.dimension_prompts_remove",
   131→    )
   132→    if remove_prompts:
   133→        prompts = dict(out.get("dimension_prompts", {}))
   134→        for dim_name in remove_prompts:
   135→            prompts.pop(dim_name, None)
   136→        out["dimension_prompts"] = prompts
   137→
   138→    if "system_prompt" in override_payload:
   139→        out["system_prompt"] = override_payload["system_prompt"]
   140→    if "system_prompt_append" in override_payload:
   141→        suffix = override_payload["system_prompt_append"]
   142→        if not isinstance(suffix, str):
   143→            raise ValueError(f"{context}.system_prompt_append must be a string")
   144→        current = out.get("system_prompt", "")
   145→        sep = "\n\n" if current and suffix else ""
   146→        out["system_prompt"] = f"{current}{sep}{suffix}"
   147→
   148→    return out
   149→
   150→
   151→def _load_payload_for_lang(
   152→    lang_name: str,
   153→    filename: str,
   154→    *,
   155→    dims_key: str,
   156→) -> tuple[dict, str]:
   157→    """Load payload for a language using shared-base + optional language overlay.
   158→
   159→    Resolution order:
   160→    1) Start from shared base payload in ``lang/framework/review_data``.
   161→    2) If language override payload exists, patch the selected base.
   162→    """
   163→    base_payload = _load_json_payload(filename)
   164→    context = filename
   165→
   166→    lang_override_path = _lang_payload_path(lang_name, _override_filename(filename))
   167→    if lang_override_path.is_file():
   168→        override_payload = _load_json_payload_from_path(lang_override_path)
   169→        base_payload = _apply_dimensions_override(
   170→            base_payload,
   171→            override_payload,
   172→            dims_key=dims_key,
   173→            context=str(lang_override_path),
   174→        )
   175→        context = f"{context} + {lang_override_path}"
   176→
   177→    return base_payload, context
   178→
   179→
   180→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   181→# Canonical loaders — use these for new code.
   182→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   183→
   184→
   185→@lru_cache(maxsize=1)
   186→def load_dimensions() -> tuple[list[str], dict[str, dict[str, object]], str]:
   187→    """Load and validate the unified review dimension definitions."""
   188→    payload = _load_json_payload(_DIMENSIONS_FILE)
   189→    dims, prompts, system_prompt = parse_dimensions_payload(
   190→        payload, context_prefix=_DIMENSIONS_FILE
   191→    )
   192→    return dims, prompts, ensure_prompt_contract(system_prompt)
   193→
   194→
   195→@lru_cache(maxsize=16)
   196→def load_dimensions_for_lang(
   197→    lang_name: str,
   198→) -> tuple[list[str], dict[str, dict[str, object]], str]:
   199→    """Load unified review dimensions for a language (with lang override applied)."""
   200→    payload, context = _load_payload_for_lang(
   201→        lang_name,
   202→        _DIMENSIONS_FILE,
   203→        [REDACTED],
   204→    )
   205→    dims, prompts, system_prompt = parse_dimensions_payload(
   206→        payload, context_prefix=context
   207→    )
   208→    return dims, prompts, ensure_prompt_contract(system_prompt)
   209→
```

```
1→"""Dimension selection policy for review preparation.
     2→
     3→This module exists to keep precedence rules explicit and centralized.
     4→"""
     5→
     6→from __future__ import annotations
     7→
     8→from desloppify.intelligence.review.dimensions.holistic import DIMENSIONS
     9→from desloppify.intelligence.review.dimensions.lang import HOLISTIC_DIMENSIONS_BY_LANG
    10→
    11→
    12→def _non_empty(values: list[str] | None) -> list[str] | None:
    13→    """Return ``values`` only when it is a non-empty list."""
    14→    if values and isinstance(values, list):
    15→        return values
    16→    return None
    17→
    18→
    19→def resolve_dimensions(
    20→    *,
    21→    cli_dimensions: list[str] | None,
    22→    lang_name: str | None = None,
    23→    config_dimensions: list[str] | None = None,
    24→    default_dimensions: list[str] | None = None,
    25→) -> list[str]:
    26→    """Resolve review dimensions using a single precedence policy.
    27→
    28→    Precedence (highest to lowest):
    29→    1) CLI ``--dimensions``
    30→    2) Language-curated defaults (if *lang_name* provided)
    31→    3) Config ``review_dimensions``
    32→    4) Caller-supplied *default_dimensions*
    33→    5) Global defaults from ``dimensions.json``
    34→    """
    35→    lang_dims = (
    36→        _non_empty(HOLISTIC_DIMENSIONS_BY_LANG.get(lang_name))
    37→        if lang_name
    38→        else None
    39→    )
    40→    return list(
    41→        _non_empty(cli_dimensions)
    42→        or lang_dims
    43→        or _non_empty(config_dimensions)
    44→        or _non_empty(default_dimensions)
    45→        or DIMENSIONS
    46→    )
    47→
```
