```
658→def write_packet_snapshot(
   659→    packet: dict,
   660→    *,
   661→    stamp: str,
   662→    review_packet_dir: Path,
   663→    blind_path: Path,
   664→    safe_write_text_fn,
   665→) -> tuple[Path, Path]:
   666→    """Persist immutable and blind packet snapshots for runner workflows."""
   667→    review_packet_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   668→    packet_path = review_packet_dir / f"holistic_packet_{stamp}.json"
   669→    safe_write_text_fn(packet_path, json.dumps(packet, indent=2) + "\n")
   670→    blind_packet = _build_blind_packet(packet)
   671→    safe_write_text_fn(blind_path, json.dumps(blind_packet, indent=2) + "\n")
   672→    return packet_path, blind_path
   673→
   674→
   675→def _build_blind_packet(packet: dict) -> dict:
   676→    """Return a blind-review packet with score anchoring metadata removed."""
   677→    blind = deepcopy(packet)
   678→    for key in _BLIND_PACKET_DROP_KEYS:
   679→        blind.pop(key, None)
   680→
   681→    config = blind.get("config")
   682→    if isinstance(config, dict):
   683→        sanitized = _sanitize_blind_config(config)
   684→        if sanitized:
   685→            blind["config"] = sanitized
   686→        else:
   687→            blind.pop("config", None)
   688→    return blind
   689→
   690→
   691→def build_blind_packet(packet: dict) -> dict:
   692→    """Public wrapper for blind packet sanitization."""
   693→    return _build_blind_packet(packet)
   694→
   695→
   696→def _sanitize_blind_config(config: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
   697→    """Drop score/target hints from config while preserving unrelated options."""
   698→    sanitized: dict[str, Any] = {}
   699→    for key, value in config.items():
   700→        lowered = key.strip().lower()
   701→        if not lowered:
   702→            continue
   703→        if lowered in _BLIND_CONFIG_SCORE_HINT_KEYS:
   704→            continue
   705→        if "target" in lowered:
   706→            continue
   707→        if lowered.endswith("_score"):
   708→            continue
   709→        sanitized[key] = value
   710→    return sanitized
   711→
   712→
   713→def sha256_file(path: Path) -> str | None:
   714→    """Compute sha256 hex digest for path contents (or None on read failure)."""
   715→    try:
   716→        data = path.read_bytes()
   717→    except OSError:
   718→        return None
   719→    return sha256(data).hexdigest()
   720→
   721→
   722→def build_batch_import_provenance(
   723→    *,
   724→    runner: str,
   725→    blind_packet_path: Path,
   726→    run_stamp: str,
   727→    batch_indexes: list[int],
   728→) -> dict[str, Any]:
   729→    """Build provenance payload used to trust assessment-bearing imports."""
   730→    packet_hash = sha256_file(blind_packet_path)
   731→    batch_indexes_1 = sorted({int(index) + 1 for index in batch_indexes})
   732→    return {
   733→        "kind": "blind_review_batch_import",
   734→        "blind": True,
   735→        "runner": runner,
   736→        "run_stamp": run_stamp,
   737→        "created_at": datetime.now(UTC).isoformat(timespec="seconds"),
   738→        "batch_count": len(batch_indexes_1),
   739→        "batch_indexes": batch_indexes_1,
   740→        "packet_path": str(blind_packet_path),
   741→        "packet_sha256": packet_hash,
   742→    }
   743→
   744→
   745→def selected_batch_indexes(
   746→    *,
   747→    raw_selection: str | None,
   748→    batch_count: int,
   749→    parse_fn,
   750→    colorize_fn,
   751→) -> list[int]:
   752→    """Validate selected batch indexes or exit with a CLI error."""
   753→    try:
   754→        selected = parse_fn(raw_selection, batch_count)
   755→    except ValueError as exc:
   756→        print(colorize_fn(f"  Error: {exc}", "red"), file=sys.stderr)
   757→        sys.exit(2)
```

```
    39→def build_holistic_context(
    40→    path: Path,
    41→    lang: object,
    42→    state: dict,
    43→    files: list[str] | None = None,
    44→) -> dict[str, object]:
    45→    """Gather codebase-wide data for holistic review."""

    47→
    48→
    49→def build_holistic_context_model(
    50→    path: Path,
    51→    lang,
    52→    state: dict,
    53→    files: list[str] | None = None,
    54→) -> HolisticContext:
    55→    """Gather holistic context and return a typed context contract."""
    56→    selected_files = select_holistic_files(path, lang, files)
    57→
    58→    already_cached = is_file_cache_enabled()
    59→    if not already_cached:
    60→        enable_file_cache()
    61→    try:
    62→        return _build_holistic_context_inner(path, selected_files, lang, state)
    63→    finally:
    64→        if not already_cached:
    65→            disable_file_cache()
    66→
    67→
    68→def _build_holistic_context_inner(
    69→    path: Path, files: list[str], lang, state: dict
    70→) -> HolisticContext:
    71→    """Inner holistic context builder (runs with file cache enabled)."""
    72→    file_contents = _read_file_contents(files)
    73→
    74→    context = HolisticContext(
    75→        architecture=_architecture_context(lang, file_contents),
    76→        coupling=_coupling_context(file_contents),
    77→        conventions={
    78→            "naming_by_directory": _naming_conventions_context(file_contents),
    79→            "sibling_behavior": _sibling_behavior_context(file_contents, base_path=path),
    80→        },
    81→        errors={
    82→            "strategy_by_directory": _error_strategy_context(file_contents),
    83→        },
    84→        abstractions=_abstractions_context(file_contents),
    85→        dependencies=_dependencies_context(state),
    86→        testing=_testing_context(lang, state, file_contents),
    87→        api_surface=_api_surface_context(lang, file_contents),
    88→        structure=compute_structure_context(file_contents, lang),
    89→    )
    90→
    91→    auth_ctx = gather_auth_context(file_contents, rel_fn=rel)
    92→    if auth_ctx:
    93→        context.authorization = auth_ctx
    94→
    95→    ai_debt = gather_ai_debt_signals(file_contents, rel_fn=rel)
    96→    if ai_debt.get("file_signals"):
    97→        context.ai_debt_signals = ai_debt
    98→
    99→    migration = gather_migration_signals(file_contents, lang, rel_fn=rel)
   100→    if migration:
   101→        context.migration_signals = migration
   102→
   103→    context.codebase_stats = _codebase_stats(file_contents)
   104→
   105→    # Enrich with aggregated mechanical detector evidence.
   106→    evidence = gather_mechanical_evidence(state)
   107→    if evidence:
   108→        context.scan_evidence = evidence
   109→        _enrich_sections_from_evidence(context, evidence)
   110→
   111→    context.normalize_sections(strict=True)
   112→    return context
   113→
   114→
   115→def _enrich_sections_from_evidence(
   116→    context: HolisticContext, evidence: dict
   117→) -> None:
   118→    """Merge mechanical evidence into existing holistic context sections."""
   119→    if "complexity_hotspots" in evidence:
   120→        context.abstractions["complexity_hotspots"] = evidence["complexity_hotspots"]
   121→    if "error_hotspots" in evidence:
   122→        context.errors["exception_hotspots"] = evidence["error_hotspots"]
   123→    if "mutable_globals" in evidence:
   124→        context.errors["mutable_globals"] = evidence["mutable_globals"]
   125→    if "boundary_violations" in evidence:
   126→        context.coupling["boundary_violations"] = evidence["boundary_violations"]
   127→    if "deferred_import_density" in evidence:
   128→        context.dependencies["deferred_import_density"] = evidence["deferred_import_density"]
   129→    if "duplicate_clusters" in evidence:
   130→        context.conventions["duplicate_clusters"] = evidence["duplicate_clusters"]
   131→    if "naming_drift" in evidence:
   132→        context.conventions["naming_drift"] = evidence["naming_drift"]
   133→    if "flat_dir_findings" in evidence:
   134→        context.structure["flat_dir_findings"] = evidence["flat_dir_findings"]
```

```
1→"""Concern generators — mechanical findings → subjective review bridge.
     2→
     3→Concerns are ephemeral: computed on-demand from current state, never persisted.
     4→Only LLM-confirmed concerns become persistent Finding objects via review import.
     5→
     6→Generators focus on cross-cutting synthesis — bundling all signals per file so
     7→the LLM gets a complete picture, and surfacing systemic patterns across files
     8→that no single detector captures.
     9→"""
    10→
    11→from __future__ import annotations
    12→
    13→import hashlib
    14→from collections import defaultdict
    15→from dataclasses import dataclass
    16→from typing import Any, Literal, TypedDict, cast
    17→
    18→from desloppify.core.registry import JUDGMENT_DETECTORS
    19→
    20→
    21→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    22→class Concern:
    23→    """A potential design problem surfaced by mechanical signals."""
    24→
    25→    type: str  # concern classification
    26→    file: str  # primary file (relative path)
    27→    summary: str  # human-readable 1-liner
    28→    evidence: tuple[str, ...]  # supporting data points
    29→    question: str  # specific question for LLM to evaluate
    30→    fingerprint: str  # stable hash for dismissal tracking
    31→    source_findings: tuple[str, ...]  # finding IDs that triggered this
    32→
    33→
    34→class ConcernSignals(TypedDict, total=False):
    35→    """Typed signal payload extracted from mechanical findings."""
    36→
    37→    max_params: float
    38→    max_nesting: float
    39→    loc: float
    40→    function_count: float
    41→    monster_loc: float
    42→    monster_funcs: list[str]
    43→
    44→
    45→_NUMERIC_SIGNAL_KEYS = ("max_params", "max_nesting", "loc", "function_count")
    46→SignalKey = Literal["max_params", "max_nesting", "loc", "function_count", "monster_loc"]
    47→
    48→
    49→def _update_max_signal(signals: ConcernSignals, key: SignalKey, value: object) -> None:
    50→    """Update numeric signal key with max(existing, value) when value is valid."""
    51→    if isinstance(value, bool) or not isinstance(value, int | float) or value <= 0:
    52→        return
    53→    current = float(signals.get(key, 0.0))
    54→    signals[key] = max(current, float(value))
    55→
    56→
    57→def _fingerprint(concern_type: str, file: str, key_signals: tuple[str, ...]) -> str:
    58→    """Stable hash of (type, file, sorted key signals)."""
    59→    raw = f"{concern_type}::{file}::{','.join(sorted(key_signals))}"
    60→    return hashlib.sha256(raw.encode()).hexdigest()[:16]
    61→
    62→
    63→def _is_dismissed(
    64→    dismissals: dict, fingerprint: str, source_finding_ids: tuple[str, ...]
    65→) -> bool:
    66→    """Check if a concern was previously dismissed and source findings unchanged."""
    67→    entry = dismissals.get(fingerprint)
    68→    if not isinstance(entry, dict):
    69→        return False
    70→    prev_sources = set(entry.get("source_finding_ids", []))
    71→    return prev_sources == set(source_finding_ids)
    72→
    73→
    74→def _open_findings(state: dict[str, Any]) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
    75→    """Return all open findings from state."""
    76→    findings = state.get("findings", {})
    77→    return [
    78→        f for f in findings.values()
    79→        if isinstance(f, dict) and f.get("status") == "open"
    80→    ]
    81→
    82→
    83→def _group_by_file(state: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, list[dict[str, Any]]]:
    84→    """Group open findings by file, excluding holistic (file='.')."""
    85→    by_file: dict[str, list[dict[str, Any]]] = defaultdict(list)
    86→    for f in _open_findings(state):
    87→        file = f.get("file", "")
    88→        if file and file != ".":
    89→            by_file[file].append(f)
    90→    return dict(by_file)
    91→
    92→
    93→# ── Signal extraction ────────────────────────────────────────────────
    94→
    95→
    96→def _extract_signals(findings: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> ConcernSignals:
    97→    """Extract key quantitative signals from a file's findings."""
    98→    signals: ConcernSignals = {}
    99→    monster_funcs: list[str] = []
   100→
   101→    for f in findings:
   102→        det = f.get("detector", "")
   103→        detail_raw = f.get("detail", {})
   104→        detail = detail_raw if isinstance(detail_raw, dict) else {}
   105→
   106→        if det == "structural":
   107→            s = detail.get("signals", {})
   108→            if isinstance(s, dict):
   109→                for key in _NUMERIC_SIGNAL_KEYS:
   110→                    _update_max_signal(signals, cast(SignalKey, key), s.get(key, 0))
   111→
   112→        if det == "smells" and detail.get("smell_id") == "monster_function":
   113→            _update_max_signal(signals, "monster_loc", detail.get("loc", 0))
   114→            func = detail.get("function", "")
   115→            if isinstance(func, str) and func:
   116→                monster_funcs.append(func)
   117→
   118→    if monster_funcs:
   119→        signals["monster_funcs"] = monster_funcs
   120→    return signals
   121→
   122→
   123→def _has_elevated_signals(findings: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> bool:
   124→    """Does any finding have signals strong enough to flag on its own?"""
   125→    for f in findings:
   126→        det = f.get("detector", "")
   127→        detail = f.get("detail", {})
   128→
   129→        if det == "structural":
   130→            s = detail.get("signals", {})
   131→            if isinstance(s, dict):
   132→                if s.get("max_params", 0) >= 8:
   133→                    return True
   134→                if s.get("max_nesting", 0) >= 6:
   135→                    return True
   136→                if s.get("loc", 0) >= 300:
   137→                    return True
   138→
   139→        if det == "smells" and detail.get("smell_id") == "monster_function":
   140→            return True
   141→
   142→        if det in ("dupes", "boilerplate_duplication", "coupling",
   143→                    "responsibility_cohesion"):
   144→            return True
   145→
   146→    return False
   147→
   148→
   149→# ── Concern classification ───────────────────────────────────────────
   150→
   151→
   152→def _classify(detectors: set[str], signals: ConcernSignals) -> str:
   153→    """Pick the most specific concern type from what's present."""
   154→    if len(detectors) >= 3:
   155→        return "mixed_responsibilities"
   156→    if "dupes" in detectors or "boilerplate_duplication" in detectors:
   157→        return "duplication_design"
   158→    if signals.get("monster_loc", 0) > 0:
   159→        return "structural_complexity"
   160→    if "coupling" in detectors:
   161→        return "coupling_design"
   162→    if signals.get("max_params", 0) >= 8:
   163→        return "interface_design"
   164→    if signals.get("max_nesting", 0) >= 6:
   165→        return "structural_complexity"
   166→    if "responsibility_cohesion" in detectors:
   167→        return "mixed_responsibilities"
   168→    return "design_concern"
   169→
   170→
   171→def _build_summary(
   172→    concern_type: str,
   173→    detectors: set[str],
   174→    signals: ConcernSignals,
   175→) -> str:
   176→    """Human-readable one-liner."""
   177→    if concern_type == "mixed_responsibilities":
   178→        return (
   179→            f"Issues from {len(detectors)} detectors — "
   180→            "may have too many responsibilities"
   181→        )
   182→    if concern_type == "structural_complexity":
   183→        parts: list[str] = []
   184→        monster_loc = signals.get("monster_loc", 0)
   185→        if monster_loc:
   186→            funcs = signals.get("monster_funcs", [])
   187→            label = f" ({', '.join(funcs[:3])})" if funcs else ""
   188→            parts.append(f"monster function{label}: {int(monster_loc)} lines")
   189→        nesting = signals.get("max_nesting", 0)
   190→        if nesting >= 6:
   191→            parts.append(f"nesting depth {int(nesting)}")
   192→        params = signals.get("max_params", 0)
   193→        if params >= 8:
   194→            parts.append(f"{int(params)} parameters")
   195→        return f"Structural complexity: {', '.join(parts) or 'elevated signals'}"
   196→    if concern_type == "duplication_design":
   197→        return "Duplication pattern — assess if extraction is warranted"
   198→    if concern_type == "coupling_design":
   199→        return "Coupling pattern — assess if boundaries need adjustment"
   200→    if concern_type == "interface_design":
   201→        return f"Interface complexity: {int(signals.get('max_params', 0))} parameters"
   202→    return f"Design signals from {', '.join(sorted(detectors))}"
   203→
   204→
   205→def _build_evidence(
   206→    findings: list[dict[str, Any]],
   207→    signals: ConcernSignals,
   208→) -> tuple[str, ...]:
   209→    """Build evidence tuple from all findings and extracted signals."""
   210→    evidence: list[str] = []
   211→
   212→    detectors = sorted({f.get("detector", "") for f in findings})
   213→    evidence.append(f"Flagged by: {', '.join(detectors)}")
   214→
   215→    loc = signals.get("loc")
   216→    if loc:
   217→        evidence.append(f"File size: {int(loc)} lines")
   218→    params = signals.get("max_params")
   219→    if params and params >= 8:
   220→        evidence.append(f"Max parameters: {int(params)}")
   221→    nesting = signals.get("max_nesting")
   222→    if nesting and nesting >= 6:
   223→        evidence.append(f"Max nesting depth: {int(nesting)}")
   224→    monster_loc = signals.get("monster_loc")
   225→    if monster_loc:
   226→        funcs = signals.get("monster_funcs", [])
   227→        label = f" ({', '.join(funcs[:3])})" if funcs else ""
   228→        evidence.append(f"Monster function{label}: {int(monster_loc)} lines")
   229→
   230→    # Individual finding summaries — give LLM the full picture, capped.
   231→    for f in findings[:10]:
   232→        summary = f.get("summary", "")
   233→        if summary:
   234→            evidence.append(f"[{f.get('detector', '')}] {summary}")
   235→
   236→    return tuple(evidence)
   237→
   238→
   239→def _build_question(
   240→    detectors: set[str], signals: ConcernSignals
   241→) -> str:
   242→    """Build targeted question from dominant signals."""
   243→    parts: list[str] = []
   244→
   245→    if len(detectors) >= 3:
   246→        parts.append(
   247→            f"This file has issues across {len(detectors)} dimensions "
   248→            f"({', '.join(sorted(detectors))}). Is it trying to do too many "
   249→            "things, or is this complexity inherent to its domain?"
   250→        )
   251→
   252→    funcs = signals.get("monster_funcs", [])
   253→    if funcs:
   254→        parts.append(
   255→            f"What are the distinct responsibilities in {funcs[0]}()? "
   256→            "Should it be decomposed into focused functions?"
   257→        )
   258→
   259→    if signals.get("max_params", 0) >= 8:
   260→        parts.append(
   261→            "Should the parameters be grouped into a config/context object? "
   262→            "Which ones belong together?"
   263→        )
   264→
   265→    if signals.get("max_nesting", 0) >= 6:
   266→        parts.append(
   267→            "Can the nesting be reduced with early returns, guard clauses, "
   268→            "or extraction into helper functions?"
   269→        )
   270→
   271→    if "dupes" in detectors or "boilerplate_duplication" in detectors:
   272→        parts.append(
   273→            "Is the duplication worth extracting into a shared utility, "
   274→            "or is it intentional variation?"
   275→        )
   276→
   277→    if "coupling" in detectors:
   278→        parts.append(
   279→            "Is the coupling intentional or does it indicate a missing "
   280→            "abstraction boundary?"
   281→        )
   282→
   283→    if "orphaned" in detectors:
   284→        parts.append(
   285→            "Is this file truly dead, or is it used via a non-import mechanism "
   286→            "(dynamic import, CLI entry point, plugin)?"
   287→        )
   288→
   289→    if "responsibility_cohesion" in detectors:
   290→        parts.append(
   291→            "What are the distinct responsibilities? Should this module be "
   292→            "split along those lines?"
   293→        )
   294→
   295→    if not parts:
   296→        parts.append(
   297→            "Review the flagged patterns — are they design problems that "
   298→            "need addressing, or acceptable given the file's role?"
   299→        )
   300→
   301→    return " ".join(parts)
   302→
   303→
   304→# ── Generators ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   305→
   306→
   307→def _file_concerns(state: dict[str, Any], dismissals: dict[str, Any]) -> list[Concern]:
   308→    """Per-file design concerns from aggregated mechanical signals.
   309→
   310→    Flags a file if it has 2+ judgment-needed detectors OR a single
   311→    detector with elevated signals (monster function, high params,
   312→    deep nesting, duplication, coupling, mixed responsibilities).
   313→    Bundles ALL findings for that file so the LLM sees the full picture.
   314→    """
   315→    by_file = _group_by_file(state)
   316→    concerns: list[Concern] = []
   317→
   318→    for file, all_findings in by_file.items():
   319→        judgment = [
   320→            f for f in all_findings
   321→            if f.get("detector", "") in JUDGMENT_DETECTORS
   322→        ]
   323→        if not judgment:
   324→            continue
   325→
   326→        judgment_dets = {f.get("detector", "") for f in judgment}
   327→        elevated = _has_elevated_signals(judgment)
   328→
   329→        # Flag if 2+ judgment detectors OR 1 with elevated signals
   330→        # OR 1 judgment detector + 2 mechanical findings from any detector.
   331→        mechanical_count = len(all_findings)
   332→        if len(judgment_dets) < 2 and not elevated:
   333→            if not (len(judgment_dets) >= 1 and mechanical_count >= 3):
   334→                continue
   335→
   336→        signals = _extract_signals(judgment)
   337→        concern_type = _classify(judgment_dets, signals)
   338→        evidence = _build_evidence(judgment, signals)
   339→        question = _build_question(judgment_dets, signals)
   340→        summary = _build_summary(concern_type, judgment_dets, signals)
   341→
   342→        all_ids = tuple(sorted(f.get("id", "") for f in judgment))
   343→        fp_keys = tuple(sorted(judgment_dets))
   344→        fp = _fingerprint(concern_type, file, fp_keys)
   345→
   346→        if _is_dismissed(dismissals, fp, all_ids):
   347→            continue
   348→
   349→        concerns.append(
   350→            Concern(
   351→                type=concern_type,
   352→                file=file,
   353→                summary=summary,
   354→                evidence=evidence,
   355→                question=question,
   356→                fingerprint=fp,
   357→                source_findings=all_ids,
   358→            )
   359→        )
   360→
   361→    return concerns
   362→
   363→
   364→def _cross_file_patterns(state: dict[str, Any], dismissals: dict[str, Any]) -> list[Concern]:
   365→    """Systemic patterns: same judgment detector combo across 3+ files.
   366→
   367→    When multiple files share the same combination of detector types,
   368→    that's likely a codebase-wide pattern rather than isolated issues.
   369→    """
   370→    by_file = _group_by_file(state)
   371→
   372→    # Group files by their judgment detector profile.
   373→    profile_to_files: dict[frozenset[str], list[str]] = defaultdict(list)
   374→    for file, findings in by_file.items():
   375→        dets = frozenset(
   376→            f.get("detector", "") for f in findings
   377→            if f.get("detector", "") in JUDGMENT_DETECTORS
   378→        )
   379→        if len(dets) >= 2:
   380→            profile_to_files[dets].append(file)
   381→
   382→    concerns: list[Concern] = []
   383→    for det_combo, files in profile_to_files.items():
   384→        if len(files) < 3:
   385→            continue
   386→
   387→        sorted_files = sorted(files)
   388→        combo_names = tuple(sorted(det_combo))
   389→        all_ids = tuple(sorted(
   390→            f.get("id", "")
   391→            for file in sorted_files
   392→            for f in by_file[file]
   393→            if f.get("detector", "") in det_combo
   394→        ))
   395→        # Use first few files in fingerprint so it's stable but bounded.
   396→        fp = _fingerprint(
   397→            "systemic_pattern",
   398→            ",".join(sorted_files[:5]),
   399→            combo_names,
   400→        )
   401→
   402→        if _is_dismissed(dismissals, fp, all_ids):
   403→            continue
   404→
   405→        concerns.append(
   406→            Concern(
   407→                type="systemic_pattern",
   408→                file=sorted_files[0],
   409→                summary=(
   410→                    f"{len(files)} files share the same problem pattern "
   411→                    f"({', '.join(combo_names)})"
   412→                ),
   413→                evidence=(
   414→                    f"Affected files: {', '.join(sorted_files[:10])}",
   415→                    f"Shared detectors: {', '.join(combo_names)}",
   416→                    f"Total files: {len(files)}",
   417→                ),
   418→                question=(
   419→                    f"These {len(files)} files all have the same combination "
   420→                    f"of issues ({', '.join(combo_names)}). Is this a systemic "
   421→                    "pattern that should be addressed at the architecture level "
   422→                    "(shared base class, framework change, lint rule), or are "
   423→                    "these independent issues that happen to look similar?"
   424→                ),
   425→                fingerprint=fp,
   426→                source_findings=all_ids,
   427→            )
   428→        )
   429→
   430→    return concerns
   431→
   432→
   433→def _systemic_smell_patterns(
   434→    state: dict[str, Any], dismissals: dict[str, Any]
   435→) -> list[Concern]:
   436→    """Systemic concerns: single smell_id appearing across 5+ files.
   437→
   438→    Complements _cross_file_patterns which looks at detector-combo profiles.
   439→    This catches pervasive single-smell issues (e.g. broad_except in 12 files).
   440→    """
   441→    smell_files: dict[str, list[str]] = defaultdict(list)
   442→    smell_ids_map: dict[str, list[str]] = defaultdict(list)  # smell_id -> finding IDs
   443→
   444→    for f in _open_findings(state):
   445→        if f.get("detector") != "smells":
   446→            continue
   447→        detail = f.get("detail", {})
   448→        smell_id = detail.get("smell_id", "") if isinstance(detail, dict) else ""
   449→        filepath = f.get("file", "")
   450→        if smell_id and filepath and filepath != ".":
   451→            smell_files[smell_id].append(filepath)
   452→            smell_ids_map[smell_id].append(f.get("id", ""))
   453→
   454→    concerns: list[Concern] = []
   455→    for smell_id, files in smell_files.items():
   456→        unique_files = sorted(set(files))
   457→        if len(unique_files) < 5:
   458→            continue
   459→
   460→        all_ids = tuple(sorted(smell_ids_map[smell_id]))
   461→        fp = _fingerprint("systemic_smell", smell_id, (smell_id,))
   462→        if _is_dismissed(dismissals, fp, all_ids):
   463→            continue
   464→
   465→        concerns.append(
   466→            Concern(
   467→                type="systemic_smell",
   468→                file=unique_files[0],
   469→                summary=(
   470→                    f"'{smell_id}' appears in {len(unique_files)} files — "
   471→                    "likely a systemic pattern"
   472→                ),
   473→                evidence=(
   474→                    f"Smell: {smell_id}",
   475→                    f"Affected files ({len(unique_files)}): {', '.join(unique_files[:10])}",
   476→                ),
   477→                question=(
   478→                    f"The smell '{smell_id}' appears across {len(unique_files)} files. "
   479→                    "Is this a codebase-wide convention that should be addressed "
   480→                    "systemically (lint rule, shared utility, architecture change), "
   481→                    "or are these independent occurrences?"
   482→                ),
   483→                fingerprint=fp,
   484→                source_findings=all_ids,
   485→            )
   486→        )
   487→
   488→    return concerns
   489→
   490→
   491→_GENERATORS = [_file_concerns, _cross_file_patterns, _systemic_smell_patterns]
   492→
   493→
   494→def generate_concerns(
   495→    state: dict[str, Any],
   496→    lang_name: str | None = None,
   497→) -> list[Concern]:
   498→    """Run all concern generators against current state.
   499→
   500→    Returns deduplicated list sorted by (type, file).
   501→    lang_name is reserved for future language-specific generators.
   502→    """
   503→    del lang_name  # Reserved for future use.
   504→    dismissals = state.get("concern_dismissals", {})
   505→    concerns: list[Concern] = []
   506→    seen_fps: set[str] = set()
   507→
   508→    for gen in _GENERATORS:
   509→        for concern in gen(state, dismissals):
   510→            if concern.fingerprint not in seen_fps:
   511→                seen_fps.add(concern.fingerprint)
   512→                concerns.append(concern)
   513→
   514→    concerns.sort(key=lambda c: (c.type, c.file))
   515→    return concerns
   516→
   517→
   518→def cleanup_stale_dismissals(state: dict[str, Any]) -> int:
   519→    """Remove dismissals whose source findings all disappeared.
   520→
   521→    Returns the number of stale entries removed.  Dismissals without
   522→    ``source_finding_ids`` (legacy) are left untouched.
   523→    """
   524→    dismissals = state.get("concern_dismissals", {})
   525→    if not dismissals:
   526→        return 0
   527→    open_ids = {f.get("id", "") for f in _open_findings(state)}
   528→    stale_fps = [
   529→        fp
   530→        for fp, entry in dismissals.items()
   531→        if entry.get("source_finding_ids")
   532→        and not any(sid in open_ids for sid in entry["source_finding_ids"])
   533→    ]
   534→    for fp in stale_fps:
   535→        del dismissals[fp]
   536→    return len(stale_fps)
   537→
   538→
   539→__all__ = ["Concern", "cleanup_stale_dismissals", "generate_concerns"]
   540→
```

```
     6→"""
     7→
     8→from __future__ import annotations

    10→from collections import Counter, defaultdict
    11→from typing import Any

    13→
    14→def gather_mechanical_evidence(state: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
    15→    """Aggregate open findings into evidence clusters for holistic review.

    17→    Returns a dict of named clusters.  Empty dict when no findings exist.

   119→
   120→# ── Cluster builders ─────────────────────────────────────────────────
   121→
   122→
   123→def _get_detail(finding: dict, key: str, default: Any = None) -> Any:
   124→    detail = finding.get("detail", {})
   125→    if not isinstance(detail, dict):
   126→        return default
   127→    return detail.get(key, default)
   128→
   129→
   130→def _get_signals(finding: dict) -> dict:
   131→    detail = finding.get("detail", {})
   132→    if not isinstance(detail, dict):
   133→        return {}
   134→    signals = detail.get("signals", {})
   135→    return signals if isinstance(signals, dict) else {}
   136→
   137→
   138→def _safe_num(value: Any, default: float = 0.0) -> float:
   139→    if isinstance(value, bool):
   140→        return default
   141→    if isinstance(value, int | float):
   142→        return float(value)
   143→    return default
   144→
   145→
   146→def _build_complexity_hotspots(
   147→    by_detector: dict[str, list[dict]],
   148→    by_file: dict[str, list[dict]],
   149→) -> list[dict]:
   150→    """Top 20 files by composite complexity score."""
   151→    # Gather per-file structural signals
   152→    file_data: dict[str, dict[str, Any]] = {}
   153→
   154→    for finding in by_detector.get("structural", []):
   155→        filepath = finding.get("file", "")
   156→        if not filepath:
   157→            continue
   158→        signals = _get_signals(finding)
   159→        entry = file_data.setdefault(filepath, {
   160→            "file": filepath, "loc": 0, "complexity_score": 0,
   161→            "signals": [], "component_count": 0, "function_count": 0,
   162→            "monster_functions": 0, "cyclomatic_hotspots": 0,
   163→        })
   164→        entry["loc"] = max(entry["loc"], _safe_num(signals.get("loc")))
   165→        entry["function_count"] = max(
   166→            entry["function_count"], _safe_num(signals.get("function_count"))
   167→        )
   168→        entry["component_count"] = max(
   169→            entry["component_count"], _safe_num(signals.get("component_count"))
   170→        )
   171→
   172→        # Collect notable signals
   173→        max_params = _safe_num(signals.get("max_params"))
   174→        if max_params >= 5:
   175→            entry["signals"].append(f"{int(max_params)} params")
   176→        max_nesting = _safe_num(signals.get("max_nesting"))
   177→        if max_nesting >= 4:
   178→            entry["signals"].append(f"nesting depth {int(max_nesting)}")
   179→        complexity = _safe_num(signals.get("complexity_score"))
   180→        entry["complexity_score"] = max(entry["complexity_score"], complexity)
   181→
   182→    # Enrich with smell findings for the same files
   183→    for finding in by_detector.get("smells", []):
   184→        filepath = finding.get("file", "")
   185→        smell_id = _get_detail(finding, "smell_id", "")
   186→        if filepath in file_data:
   187→            if smell_id == "monster_function":
   188→                file_data[filepath]["monster_functions"] += 1
   189→            elif smell_id in ("cyclomatic_complexity", "high_cyclomatic"):
   190→                file_data[filepath]["cyclomatic_hotspots"] += 1
   191→
   192→    # Also count responsibility_cohesion findings
   193→    for finding in by_detector.get("responsibility_cohesion", []):
   194→        filepath = finding.get("file", "")
   195→        if filepath in file_data:
   196→            clusters = _safe_num(_get_detail(finding, "cluster_count"))
   197→            file_data[filepath]["component_count"] = max(
   198→                file_data[filepath]["component_count"], clusters
   199→            )
   200→
   201→    # Composite score: loc/100 + complexity_score + component_count*3 + monster*10
   202→    for entry in file_data.values():
   203→        entry["_score"] = (
   204→            entry["loc"] / 100
   205→            + entry["complexity_score"]
   206→            + entry["component_count"] * 3
   207→            + entry["monster_functions"] * 10
   208→        )
   209→        # Deduplicate signals
   210→        entry["signals"] = list(dict.fromkeys(entry["signals"]))
   211→
   212→    ranked = sorted(file_data.values(), key=lambda e: -e["_score"])[:20]
   213→    for entry in ranked:
   214→        del entry["_score"]
   215→    return ranked
   216→
   217→
   218→def _build_error_hotspots(by_detector: dict[str, list[dict]]) -> list[dict]:
   219→    """Files with 3+ exception handling findings from smells detector."""
   220→    error_smell_ids = frozenset({
   221→        "broad_except", "silent_except", "empty_except",
   222→        "swallowed_error", "bare_except",
   223→    })
   224→    file_counts: dict[str, dict[str, int]] = defaultdict(lambda: defaultdict(int))
   225→
   226→    for finding in by_detector.get("smells", []):
   227→        smell_id = _get_detail(finding, "smell_id", "")
   228→        if smell_id not in error_smell_ids:
   229→            continue
   230→        filepath = finding.get("file", "")
   231→        if filepath:
   232→            file_counts[filepath][smell_id] += 1
   233→
   234→    results = []
   235→    for filepath, counts in file_counts.items():
   236→        total = sum(counts.values())
   237→        if total < 3:
   238→            continue
   239→        entry = {"file": filepath, "total": total}
   240→        for sid in sorted(error_smell_ids):
   241→            entry[sid] = counts.get(sid, 0)
   242→        results.append(entry)
   243→    results.sort(key=lambda e: -e["total"])
   244→    return results[:20]
   245→
   246→
   247→def _build_mutable_globals(by_detector: dict[str, list[dict]]) -> list[dict]:
   248→    """All global_mutable_config findings."""
   249→    results: list[dict] = []
   250→    file_data: dict[str, dict] = {}
   251→
   252→    for finding in by_detector.get("global_mutable_config", []):
   253→        filepath = finding.get("file", "")
   254→        if not filepath:
   255→            continue
   256→        entry = file_data.setdefault(filepath, {
   257→            "file": filepath, "names": [], "total_mutations": 0,
   258→        })
   259→        name = _get_detail(finding, "name", "")
   260→        if name and name not in entry["names"]:
   261→            entry["names"].append(name)
   262→        mutations = _safe_num(_get_detail(finding, "mutations"))
   263→        entry["total_mutations"] += int(mutations) if mutations else 1
   264→
   265→    results = sorted(file_data.values(), key=lambda e: -e["total_mutations"])
   266→    return results[:20]
   267→
   268→
   269→def _build_boundary_violations(by_detector: dict[str, list[dict]]) -> list[dict]:
   270→    """From coupling and layer_violation detectors."""
   271→    results: list[dict] = []
   272→    for det_name in ("coupling", "layer_violation"):
   273→        for finding in by_detector.get(det_name, []):
   274→            filepath = finding.get("file", "")
   275→            detail = finding.get("detail", {})
   276→            if not isinstance(detail, dict):
   277→                detail = {}
   278→            results.append({
   279→                "file": filepath,
   280→                "target": detail.get("target", detail.get("imported_from", "")),
   281→                "direction": detail.get("direction", detail.get("violation", "")),
   282→            })
   283→    return results[:30]
   284→
   285→
   286→def _build_dead_code(by_detector: dict[str, list[dict]]) -> list[dict]:
   287→    """Orphaned files + uncalled functions."""
   288→    results: list[dict] = []
   289→    for finding in by_detector.get("orphaned", []):
   290→        filepath = finding.get("file", "")
   291→        signals = _get_signals(finding)
   292→        loc = _safe_num(signals.get("loc", _get_detail(finding, "loc")))
   293→        results.append({"file": filepath, "kind": "orphaned", "loc": int(loc)})
   294→    for finding in by_detector.get("uncalled_functions", []):
   295→        filepath = finding.get("file", "")
   296→        detail = finding.get("detail", {})
   297→        loc = _safe_num(detail.get("loc", 0)) if isinstance(detail, dict) else 0
   298→        results.append({"file": filepath, "kind": "uncalled", "loc": int(loc)})
   299→    return results[:30]
   300→
   301→
   302→def _build_private_crossings(by_detector: dict[str, list[dict]]) -> list[dict]:
   303→    """From private_imports detector."""
   304→    results: list[dict] = []
   305→    for finding in by_detector.get("private_imports", []):
   306→        filepath = finding.get("file", "")
   307→        detail = finding.get("detail", {})
   308→        if not isinstance(detail, dict):
   309→            detail = {}
   310→        results.append({
   311→            "file": filepath,
   312→            "symbol": detail.get("symbol", detail.get("name", "")),
   313→            "source": detail.get("source", detail.get("imported_from", "")),
   314→            "target": detail.get("target", filepath),
   315→        })
   316→    return results[:30]
   317→
   318→
   319→def _build_deferred_import_density(by_file: dict[str, list[dict]]) -> list[dict]:
   320→    """Files with 2+ deferred_import smells (proxy for cycle pressure)."""
   321→    file_counts: dict[str, int] = defaultdict(int)
   322→    for filepath, file_findings in by_file.items():
   323→        for finding in file_findings:
   324→            if finding.get("detector") != "smells":
   325→                continue
   326→            if _get_detail(finding, "smell_id") == "deferred_import":
   327→                file_counts[filepath] += 1
   328→
   329→    results = [
   330→        {"file": filepath, "count": count}
   331→        for filepath, count in file_counts.items()
   332→        if count >= 2
   333→    ]
   334→    results.sort(key=lambda e: -e["count"])
   335→    return results[:20]
   336→
   337→
   338→def _build_duplicate_clusters(by_detector: dict[str, list[dict]]) -> list[dict]:
   339→    """From dupes and boilerplate_duplication detectors."""
   340→    results: list[dict] = []
   341→    for det_name in ("dupes", "boilerplate_duplication"):
   342→        for finding in by_detector.get(det_name, []):
   343→            detail = finding.get("detail", {})
   344→            if not isinstance(detail, dict):
   345→                detail = {}
   346→            kind = detail.get("kind", det_name)
   347→            name = detail.get("name", detail.get("function", finding.get("summary", "")[:60]))
   348→            files = detail.get("files", [])
   349→            if not isinstance(files, list):
   350→                files = [finding.get("file", "")]
   351→            results.append({
   352→                "kind": kind,
   353→                "cluster_size": len(files) if files else 1,
   354→                "name": name,
   355→                "files": files[:10],
   356→            })
   357→    return results[:20]
   358→
   359→
   360→def _build_naming_drift(by_detector: dict[str, list[dict]]) -> list[dict]:
   361→    """From naming detector."""
   362→    dir_data: dict[str, dict] = {}
   363→    for finding in by_detector.get("naming", []):
   364→        filepath = finding.get("file", "")
   365→        detail = finding.get("detail", {})
   366→        if not isinstance(detail, dict):
   367→            detail = {}
   368→        # Group by directory
   369→        parts = filepath.rsplit("/", 1)
   370→        directory = parts[0] + "/" if len(parts) > 1 else "./"
   371→        entry = dir_data.setdefault(directory, {
   372→            "directory": directory,
   373→            "majority": detail.get("expected_convention", ""),
   374→            "minority_count": 0,
   375→            "outliers": [],
   376→        })
   377→        entry["minority_count"] += 1
   378→        if filepath not in entry["outliers"]:
   379→            entry["outliers"].append(filepath)
   380→        if not entry["majority"] and detail.get("expected_convention"):
   381→            entry["majority"] = detail["expected_convention"]
   382→
   383→    results = sorted(dir_data.values(), key=lambda e: -e["minority_count"])
   384→    return results[:20]
   385→
   386→
   387→def _build_flat_dir_findings(by_detector: dict[str, list[dict]]) -> list[dict]:
   388→    """From flat_dirs detector."""
   389→    results: list[dict] = []
   390→    for finding in by_detector.get("flat_dirs", []):
   391→        filepath = finding.get("file", "")
   392→        detail = finding.get("detail", {})
   393→        if not isinstance(detail, dict):
   394→            detail = {}
   395→        results.append({
   396→            "directory": filepath,
   397→            "kind": detail.get("kind", detail.get("reason", "")),
   398→            "file_count": int(_safe_num(detail.get("file_count"))),
   399→            "combined_score": int(_safe_num(detail.get("score", detail.get("combined_score")))),
   400→        })
   401→    results.sort(key=lambda e: -e["combined_score"])
   402→    return results[:20]
   403→
   404→
   405→def _build_large_file_distribution(by_detector: dict[str, list[dict]]) -> dict | None:
   406→    """Distribution stats from structural findings."""
   407→    locs: list[float] = []
   408→    for finding in by_detector.get("structural", []):
   409→        signals = _get_signals(finding)
   410→        loc = _safe_num(signals.get("loc"))
   411→        if loc > 0:
   412→            locs.append(loc)
   413→    if not locs:
   414→        return None
   415→    locs.sort()
   416→    n = len(locs)
   417→    return {
   418→        "count": n,
   419→        "median_loc": int(locs[n // 2]),
   420→        "p90_loc": int(locs[int(n * 0.9)]) if n >= 10 else int(locs[-1]),
   421→        "p99_loc": int(locs[int(n * 0.99)]) if n >= 100 else int(locs[-1]),
   422→    }
   423→
   424→
   425→def _build_security_hotspots(by_detector: dict[str, list[dict]]) -> list[dict]:
   426→    """Files with 3+ security findings grouped by severity."""
   427→    file_counts: dict[str, dict[str, int]] = defaultdict(lambda: defaultdict(int))
   428→    for finding in by_detector.get("security", []):
   429→        filepath = finding.get("file", "")
   430→        if not filepath:
   431→            continue
   432→        detail = finding.get("detail", {})
   433→        severity = (
   434→            detail.get("severity", "medium")
   435→            if isinstance(detail, dict) else "medium"
   436→        )
   437→        file_counts[filepath][severity] += 1
   438→
   439→    results = []
   440→    for filepath, counts in file_counts.items():
   441→        total = sum(counts.values())
   442→        if total < 3:
   443→            continue
   444→        results.append({
   445→            "file": filepath,
   446→            "high_severity": counts.get("high", 0),
   447→            "medium_severity": counts.get("medium", 0),
   448→            "total": total,
   449→        })
   450→    results.sort(key=lambda e: (-e["high_severity"], -e["total"]))
   451→    return results[:20]
   452→
   453→
   454→def _build_signal_density(by_file: dict[str, list[dict]]) -> list[dict]:
   455→    """Top 20 files by number of distinct detectors firing."""
   456→    results: list[dict] = []
   457→    for filepath, file_findings in by_file.items():
   458→        detectors = set()
   459→        for finding in file_findings:
   460→            det = finding.get("detector", "")
   461→            if det:
   462→                detectors.add(det)
   463→        if len(detectors) >= 2:
   464→            results.append({
   465→                "file": filepath,
   466→                "detector_count": len(detectors),
   467→                "finding_count": len(file_findings),
   468→                "detectors": sorted(detectors),
   469→            })
   470→    results.sort(key=lambda e: (-e["detector_count"], -e["finding_count"]))
   471→    return results[:20]
   472→
   473→
   474→def _build_systemic_patterns(
   475→    smell_counter: Counter[str],
   476→    smell_files: dict[str, list[str]],
   477→) -> list[dict]:
   478→    """Smell subtypes appearing in 5+ files."""
   479→    results: list[dict] = []
   480→    for smell_id, count in smell_counter.most_common():
   481→        unique_files = sorted(set(smell_files.get(smell_id, [])))
   482→        if len(unique_files) < 5:
   483→            continue
   484→        # Find top hotspot files (most occurrences)
   485→        file_counts = Counter(smell_files[smell_id])
   486→        hotspots = [
   487→            f"{f} ({c})" for f, c in file_counts.most_common(5)
   488→        ]
   489→        results.append({
   490→            "pattern": smell_id,
   491→            "file_count": len(unique_files),
   492→            "hotspots": hotspots,
   493→        })
   494→    return results[:20]
   495→
   496→
   497→__all__ = ["gather_mechanical_evidence"]
   498→
```

## Summary: Mechanical Evidence → Subjective Review Dimensions Mapping

Based on my analysis of the desloppify codebase, here's the complete mapping:

### 1. **All Defined Dimensions (20 total)**

From `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/_framework/review_data/dimensions.json`:

```
1. naming_quality
2. logic_clarity
3. type_safety
4. contract_coherence
5. error_consistency
6. abstraction_fitness
7. ai_generated_debt
8. high_level_elegance
9. mid_level_elegance
10. low_level_elegance
11. cross_module_architecture
12. initialization_coupling
13. convention_outlier
14. dependency_health
15. test_strategy
16. api_surface_coherence
17. authorization_consistency
18. incomplete_migration
19. package_organization
20. design_coherence
```

Plus 2 additional legacy dimensions (from metadata.py):
- `comment_quality`
- `authorization_coherence`

### 2. **Default Dimensions in Holistic Review**

The default set (enabled by default across all languages) from `dimensions.json`:
```json
"default_dimensions": [
  "naming_quality",
  "logic_clarity",
  "type_safety",
  "contract_coherence",
  "error_consistency",
  "abstraction_fitness",
  "ai_generated_debt",
  "high_level_elegance",
  "mid_level_elegance",
  "low_level_elegance",
  "cross_module_architecture",
  "initialization_coupling",
  "convention_outlier",
  "dependency_health",
  "test_strategy",
  "api_surface_coherence",
  "authorization_consistency",
  "incomplete_migration",
  "package_organization",
  "design_coherence"
]
```

### 3. **Verification: `initialization_coupling` and `design_coherence` Exist**

✅ **YES, BOTH ARE DEFINED:**

**`initialization_coupling`** (lines 205-217 of dimensions.json):
- Description: "Boot-order dependencies, import-time side effects, global singletons"
- Look for: Module-level code dependencies, import-time side effects, global singletons, environment variable reads at import time

**`design_coherence`** (lines 355-375 of dimensions.json):
- Description: "Are structural design decisions sound — functions focused, abstractions earned, patterns consistent?"
- Look for: Functions doing too many things, parameter lists that should be objects, deep nesting, repeated structural patterns

### 4. **How `filter_batches_to_dimensions()` Works**

From `prepare_batches.py` lines 511-556:

```python
def filter_batches_to_dimensions(
    batches: list[dict],
    dimensions: list[str],
    *,
    fallback_max_files: int | None = 80,
) -> list[dict]:
```

**Logic:**
1. Takes active dimension set (`dimensions`)
2. For each batch, filters to only dimensions in the active set
3. If a batch has no dimensions matching the active set, the entire batch is **skipped**
4. Tracks which dimensions are "covered" by filtered batches
5. **If dimensions remain uncovered**, appends a "Cross-cutting Sweep" fallback batch with representative files

**Key behavior:** If a batch references dimensions not in the active set, those dimensions are **dropped from that batch**. If ALL dimensions in a batch are inactive, the batch is **completely removed**.

### 5. **How `resolve_dimensions()` Works**

From `selection.py` lines 19-46:

**Precedence (highest to lowest):**
1. CLI `--dimensions` flag
2. Language-curated defaults (if lang_name provided)
3. Config `review_dimensions`
4. Caller-supplied default_dimensions
5. Global defaults from `dimensions.json`

**Per-language defaults** from `HOLISTIC_DIMENSIONS_BY_LANG`:
- Python: 12 dimensions (excludes: `type_safety`, `contract_coherence`, `logic_clarity`, `naming_quality`, `authorization_consistency`, `incomplete_migration`)
- TypeScript: 13 dimensions (excludes: `type_safety`, `contract_coherence`, `logic_clarity`, `naming_quality`, `dependency_health`)
- C#: 13 dimensions (excludes: `type_safety`, `contract_coherence`, `logic_clarity`, `naming_quality`, `dependency_health`, `test_strategy`)
- Go/Dart/GDScript: 5 dimensions (only: `cross_module_architecture`, `error_consistency`, `abstraction_fitness`, `test_strategy`, `design_coherence`)

### 6. **Mechanical Evidence → Dimension Mapping**

The bridge is **`batch_concerns()`** in `prepare_batches.py` (lines 559-602), which:

1. Takes `Concern` objects from `generate_concerns()` in `engine/concerns.py`
2. Maps them to **exactly TWO dimensions**:
   - `design_coherence`
   - `initialization_coupling`

**Concern types generated** (from `concerns.py`):
- `mixed_responsibilities` (multiple detectors flag same file)
- `structural_complexity` (monster functions, deep nesting, too many parameters)
- `duplication_design` (duplication patterns detected)
- `coupling_design` (coupling patterns detected)
- `interface_design` (high parameter count)
- `systemic_pattern` (same detector combo across 3+ files)
- `systemic_smell` (single smell_id across 5+ files)

**Evidence sources:**
- Mechanical detectors: `structural`, `smells` (monster_function), `dupes`, `boilerplate_duplication`, `coupling`, `responsibility_cohesion`
- File grouping: Concerns bundle ALL findings for a file so LLM sees complete picture
- Filtering: Only processes open findings from state, groups by file

The 8 explicit investigation batches map different mechanical signals to dimension sets:
- **Batch 1:** Architecture & Coupling → `[cross_module_architecture, high_level_elegance]`
- **Batch 2:** Conventions & Errors → `[convention_outlier, error_consistency, mid_level_elegance]`
- **Batch 3:** Abstractions & Dependencies → `[abstraction_fitness, dependency_health, mid_level_elegance, low_level_elegance]`
- **Batch 4:** Testing & API → `[test_strategy, api_surface_coherence, mid_level_elegance]`
- **Batch 5:** Authorization → `[authorization_consistency, mid_level_elegance]`
- **Batch 6:** AI Debt & Migrations → `[ai_generated_debt, incomplete_migration, low_level_elegance]`
- **Batch 7:** Package Organization → `[package_organization, high_level_elegance]`
- **Batch 8:** State & Design Integrity → `[initialization_coupling, design_coherence]`

The **concern batch (from mechanical signals)** then maps to the two "catch-all" dimensions: `[design_coherence, initialization_coupling]`.

```
55:        names.append(token)
410:        max_chain_depth = max((token.count(".") for token in chain_matches), default=0)
```

```python
# Lines 675-688: Build blind packet
def _build_blind_packet(packet: dict) -> dict:
    """Return a blind-review packet with score anchoring metadata removed."""
    blind = deepcopy(packet)
    for key in _BLIND_PACKET_DROP_KEYS:
        blind.pop(key, None)
    
    config = blind.get("config")
    if isinstance(config, dict):
        sanitized = _sanitize_blind_config(config)
        if sanitized:
            blind["config"] = sanitized
        else:
            blind.pop("config", None)
    return blind
```

```python
}
```

The batch prompt template explicitly guides the reviewer to use `scan_evidence`:

```python
def _render_scan_evidence_focus(dim_set: set[str]) -> str:
    """Render dimension-specific scan_evidence guidance."""
    parts: list[str] = []
    if "initialization_coupling" in dim_set:
        parts.append(
            "9e. For initialization_coupling, use evidence from "
            "`holistic_context.scan_evidence.mutable_globals` and "
            "`holistic_context.errors.mutable_globals` ..."
        )
    if "design_coherence" in dim_set:
        parts.append(

            "`holistic_context.scan_evidence.signal_density` — files where "
            "multiple mechanical detectors fired ..."
        )
    ...
```

**How the reviewer knows about more signals:**

1. **Signal density guidance** (lines 29-32): Points reviewer to `scan_evidence.signal_density` to find files where multiple detectors fired
2. **Complexity hotspots** (line 31): Directs to `scan_evidence.complexity_hotspots` for high responsibility cluster files
3. **Structure guidance** (lines 131-138): Ground scoring in objective structure signals from `holistic_context.structure`
4. **Abstraction guidance** (lines 142-153): Use evidence from `holistic_context.abstractions` including `complexity_hotspots`

**Answer to Q6:** Yes, there is an explicit mechanism. The batch prompt template embeds **dimension-specific guidance** that directs the reviewer to consult specific subsections of `scan_evidence` (lines 21, 29, 31, 44, 51). The prompt says:

```
"9f. For design_coherence, use evidence from 
`holistic_context.scan_evidence.signal_density` — files where 
multiple mechanical detectors fired."
```

This tells the reviewer: "Look at this specific part of the blind packet JSON; here are files with concentrated mechanical signals." The guidance essentially says: "Don't just look at seed files; follow these signal_density clusters to find more high-signal areas."

---

## Key Architecture Points

1. **Blind Packet = Full Holistic Context**: The blind packet contains the complete `holistic_context` dict (with all `scan_evidence` clusters) minus only score-anchoring metadata.

2. **No Size Limits**: There's no token counting or budget enforcement on how much holistic context gets passed. The evidence is aggregated deterministically and written to JSON without truncation (though individual clusters have static max items).

3. **Three Layers of Guidance**:
   - **Seed files**: Explicit batch file list 
   - **Signal density**: `scan_evidence.signal_density` flags files with 2+ detectors firing
   - **Dimension-specific**: Lines 21-54 in batch_prompt_template.py tell reviewer exactly which evidence subsections to consult for each dimension

4. **Evidence Enrichment**: Mechanical evidence is not just added to `scan_evidence`—it's also merged into domain-specific sections (e.g., `complexity_hotspots` → `abstractions`, `error_hotspots` → `errors`, `flat_dir_findings` → `structure`). This creates redundancy so reviewers can navigate evidence multiple ways.

```
    62→def parse_batch_selection(raw: str | None, batch_count: int) -> list[int]:
    63→    """Parse optional 1-based CSV list of batches."""
    64→    if not raw:
    65→        return list(range(batch_count))
    66→
    67→    selected: list[int] = []
    68→    seen: set[int] = set()
    69→    for token in raw.split(","):
    70→        text = token.strip()
    71→        if not text:
    72→            continue
    73→        idx_1 = int(text)
    74→        if idx_1 < 1 or idx_1 > batch_count:
    75→            raise ValueError(f"batch index {idx_1} out of range 1..{batch_count}")
    76→        idx_0 = idx_1 - 1
    77→        if idx_0 in seen:
    78→            continue
    79→        seen.add(idx_0)
    80→        selected.append(idx_0)
    81→    return selected
    82→
    83→
    84→def extract_json_payload(raw: str, *, log_fn) -> dict[str, object] | None:
    85→    """Best-effort extraction of first JSON object from agent output text."""
    86→    text = raw.strip()
    87→    if not text:
    88→        return None
    89→
    90→    decoder = json.JSONDecoder()
    91→    last_decode_error: json.JSONDecodeError | None = None
    92→    for start, ch in enumerate(text):
    93→        if ch not in "{[":
    94→            continue
    95→        try:
    96→            obj, _ = decoder.raw_decode(text[start:])
    97→        except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
    98→            last_decode_error = exc
    99→            continue
   100→        if (
   101→            isinstance(obj, dict)
   102→            and isinstance(obj.get("assessments"), dict)
   103→            and isinstance(obj.get("findings"), list)
   104→        ):
   105→            return obj
   106→    if last_decode_error is not None:
   107→        log_fn(f"  batch output JSON parse failed: {last_decode_error.msg}")
   108→    else:
   109→        log_fn("  batch output JSON parse failed: no valid payload found")
   110→    return None
   111→

   113→def _validate_dimension_note(
   114→    key: str,
   115→    note_raw: object,
   116→) -> tuple[list[object], str, str, str, str]:
   117→    """Validate a single dimension_notes entry and return parsed fields.
   118→
   119→    Returns (evidence, impact_scope, fix_scope, confidence, issues_preventing_higher_score).
   120→    Raises ValueError on invalid structure.
   121→    """
   122→    if not isinstance(note_raw, dict):
   123→        raise ValueError(
   124→            f"dimension_notes missing object for assessed dimension: {key}"
   125→        )
   126→    evidence = note_raw.get("evidence")
   127→    impact_scope = note_raw.get("impact_scope")
   128→    fix_scope = note_raw.get("fix_scope")
   129→    if not isinstance(evidence, list) or not evidence:
   130→        raise ValueError(
   131→            f"dimension_notes.{key}.evidence must be a non-empty array"
   132→        )
   133→    if not isinstance(impact_scope, str) or not impact_scope.strip():
   134→        raise ValueError(
   135→            f"dimension_notes.{key}.impact_scope must be a non-empty string"
   136→        )
   137→    if not isinstance(fix_scope, str) or not fix_scope.strip():
   138→        raise ValueError(
   139→            f"dimension_notes.{key}.fix_scope must be a non-empty string"
   140→        )
   141→
   142→    confidence_raw = str(note_raw.get("confidence", "medium")).strip().lower()
   143→    confidence = (
   144→        confidence_raw if confidence_raw in {"high", "medium", "low"} else "medium"
   145→    )
   146→    issues_note = str(note_raw.get(DIMENSION_NOTE_ISSUES_KEY, "")).strip()
   147→    if not issues_note:
   148→        issues_note = str(note_raw.get(LEGACY_DIMENSION_NOTE_ISSUES_KEY, "")).strip()
   149→    return evidence, impact_scope, fix_scope, confidence, issues_note
   150→
   151→
   152→def _normalize_abstraction_sub_axes(
   153→    note_raw: dict[str, object],
   154→    abstraction_sub_axes: tuple[str, ...],
   155→) -> dict[str, float]:
   156→    """Extract and clamp abstraction_fitness sub-axis scores from a note."""
   157→    sub_axes_raw = note_raw.get("sub_axes")
   158→    if sub_axes_raw is not None and not isinstance(sub_axes_raw, dict):
   159→        raise ValueError(
   160→            "dimension_notes.abstraction_fitness.sub_axes must be an object"
   161→        )
   162→    if not isinstance(sub_axes_raw, dict):
   163→        return {}
   164→
   165→    normalized: dict[str, float] = {}
   166→    for axis in abstraction_sub_axes:
   167→        axis_value = sub_axes_raw.get(axis)
   168→        if axis_value is None:
   169→            continue
   170→        if isinstance(axis_value, bool) or not isinstance(
   171→            axis_value, int | float
   172→        ):
   173→            raise ValueError(
   174→                f"dimension_notes.abstraction_fitness.sub_axes.{axis} "
   175→                "must be numeric"
   176→            )
   177→        normalized[axis] = round(
   178→            max(0.0, min(100.0, float(axis_value))),
   179→            1,
   180→        )
   181→    return normalized
   182→
   183→
   184→def _normalize_findings(
   185→    raw_findings: object,
   186→    dimension_notes: dict[str, dict[str, Any]],
   187→    *,
   188→    max_batch_findings: int,
   189→    allowed_dims: set[str],
   190→    low_score_dimensions: set[str] | None = None,
   191→) -> list[NormalizedBatchFinding]:
   192→    """Validate and normalize the findings array from a batch payload."""
   193→    if not isinstance(raw_findings, list):
   194→        raise ValueError("findings must be an array")
   195→
   196→    findings: list[NormalizedBatchFinding] = []
   197→    errors: list[str] = []
   198→    for idx, item in enumerate(raw_findings):
   199→        finding: ReviewFindingPayload | None
   200→        finding, finding_errors = validate_review_finding_payload(
   201→            item,
   202→            label=f"findings[{idx}]",
   203→            allowed_dimensions=allowed_dims,
   204→            allow_dismissed=False,
   205→        )
   206→        if finding_errors:
   207→            errors.extend(finding_errors)
   208→            continue
   209→        assert finding is not None
   210→
   211→        dim = finding["dimension"]
   212→        note = dimension_notes.get(dim, {})
   213→        impact_scope = str(
   214→            (item if isinstance(item, dict) else {}).get(
   215→                "impact_scope", note.get("impact_scope", "")
   216→            )
   217→        ).strip()
   218→        fix_scope = str(
   219→            (item if isinstance(item, dict) else {}).get(
   220→                "fix_scope", note.get("fix_scope", "")
   221→            )
   222→        ).strip()
   223→        if not impact_scope or not fix_scope:
   224→            errors.append(
   225→                f"findings[{idx}] requires impact_scope and fix_scope "
   226→                "(or dimension_notes defaults)"
   227→            )
   228→            continue
   229→        findings.append(
   230→            NormalizedBatchFinding(
   231→                dimension=finding["dimension"],
   232→                identifier=finding["identifier"],
   233→                summary=finding["summary"],
   234→                confidence=finding["confidence"],
   235→                suggestion=finding["suggestion"],
   236→                related_files=list(finding.get("related_files", [])),
   237→                evidence=list(finding.get("evidence", [])),
   238→                impact_scope=impact_scope,
   239→                fix_scope=fix_scope,
   240→                reasoning=str(finding.get("reasoning", "")),
   241→                evidence_lines=list(finding.get("evidence_lines", []))
   242→                if isinstance(finding.get("evidence_lines"), list)
   243→                else None,
   244→            )
   245→        )
   246→    if errors:
   247→        visible = errors[:10]
   248→        remaining = len(errors) - len(visible)
   249→        if remaining > 0:
   250→            visible.append(f"... {remaining} additional finding schema error(s) omitted")
   251→        raise ValueError("; ".join(visible))
   252→    if len(findings) <= max_batch_findings:
   253→        return findings
   254→
   255→    required_dims = set(low_score_dimensions or set())
   256→    if not required_dims:
   257→        return findings[:max_batch_findings]
   258→
   259→    # Preserve at least one finding per low-score dimension before trimming.
   260→    selected: list[NormalizedBatchFinding] = []
   261→    selected_indexes: set[int] = set()
   262→    covered: set[str] = set()
   263→    for idx, finding in enumerate(findings):
   264→        if len(selected) >= max_batch_findings:
   265→            break
   266→        dim = finding.dimension.strip()
   267→        if dim not in required_dims or dim in covered:
   268→            continue
   269→        selected.append(finding)
   270→        selected_indexes.add(idx)
   271→        covered.add(dim)
   272→
   273→    for idx, finding in enumerate(findings):
   274→        if len(selected) >= max_batch_findings:
   275→            break
   276→        if idx in selected_indexes:
   277→            continue
   278→        selected.append(finding)
   279→    return selected

   282→def _low_score_dimensions(assessments: dict[str, float]) -> set[str]:
   283→    """Return assessed dimensions requiring explicit defect findings."""
   284→    return {
   285→        dim
   286→        for dim, score in assessments.items()
   287→        if score < LOW_SCORE_FINDING_THRESHOLD
   288→    }
   289→
   290→
   291→def _enforce_low_score_findings(
   292→    *,
   293→    assessments: dict[str, float],
   294→    findings: list[NormalizedBatchFinding],
   295→) -> None:
   296→    """Fail closed when low scores do not report explicit findings."""
   297→    required_dims = _low_score_dimensions(assessments)
   298→    if not required_dims:
   299→        return
   300→    finding_dims = {
   301→        finding.dimension.strip() for finding in findings
   302→    }
   303→    missing = sorted(dim for dim in required_dims if dim not in finding_dims)
   304→    if not missing:
   305→        return
   306→    joined = ", ".join(missing)
   307→    raise ValueError(
   308→        "low-score dimensions must include at least one explicit finding: "
   309→        f"{joined} (threshold {LOW_SCORE_FINDING_THRESHOLD:.1f})"
   310→    )

   322→        "dimension_coverage": round(
   323→            len(assessments) / max(len(allowed_dims), 1),
   324→            3,
   325→        ),
   326→        "evidence_density": round(
   327→            sum(len(note.get("evidence", [])) for note in dimension_notes.values())
```
